325 — Mixture-of-Experts (MoE) Runtime

Project Number: 325

Project Name: Mixture-of-Experts (MoE) Runtime

Owner: Mujir Qureshi

Document: FINAL_BLUEPRINT.md

Status: specification — locked

1. Goal

Goal: Implement, from scratch in Rust, a Mixture-of-Experts (MoE) runtime that executes MoE transformer layers: a top-k gating/router (softmax-over-topk, noisy and expert-choice variants, auxiliary load-balance + router z-loss), capacity-bounded token dispatch/combine with deterministic dropping, batched/grouped expert FFN GEMM, expert parallelism with all-to-all dispatch/combine across ranks, and a comm/compute-overlap scheduler. It orchestrates MoE-layer execution (crates moe-core/gate/dispatch/expert/comm/sched/runtime/ telemetry), FFI-ing to a GEMM backend — it is not a model trainer, a tensor library, or a serving layer. Validated by differential tests against dense reference compute and against Tutel/Megablocks routing semantics.

1.1 Precise Engineering Definition

Build moe-runtime: a from-scratch Rust runtime that executes Mixture-of-Experts transformer layers in forward (and backward-compatible) pass. The system receives input tokens [n_tokens, d_model], runs sparse top-k gating, dispatches tokens to selected experts across expert-parallel (EP) ranks via all-to-all, executes per-expert FFN GEMMs, collects results back, and combines with gate weights into output [n_tokens, d_model].

Scope: forward-pass orchestration of a single MoE layer (stackable by caller). Backward (gradient) hooks are designed-in but not implemented until Phase 7. The runtime is a library — no HTTP/gRPC serving layer.

1.2 System Boundaries

In scope Out of scope
Top-k gating + softmax + noisy/expert-choice variants Full model training loop
Capacity management + token drop policy Tensor/BLAS library (FFI consumer only)
Token dispatch/combine (permute/scatter-gather) Gradient checkpointing
Expert FFN forward (batched/grouped GEMM via FFI) Dynamic batching / serving stack
All-to-all collectives across EP ranks Tokenization, embedding, attention layers
Comm/compute overlap via stream scheduling Distributed optimizer / weight update
Auxiliary load-balance loss + z-loss computation Checkpoint serialization (Phase 6)
Expert utilization / drop-rate telemetry HTTP health endpoints
moe-cli driver + moe-bench harness Kernel/autotuning (FFI delegate)

1.3 Non-Goals (Phase Deferred or Permanently Out)

  • Full model trainer (training loop, optimizer, data loader) — permanently out of scope; see §1.2 System Boundaries
  • INT4 quantized expert weights (int8/fp8 built in Phase 10) — PhaseDeferred("Phase 10")
  • Dynamic expert routing with learned capacity (runtime-adjusted top-k / capacity_factor) — PhaseDeferred("Phase 9")
  • HTTP/REST/gRPC serving layer — permanently out of scope
  • Automatic differentiation engine (general-purpose autograd; distinct from the fixed backward hooks this runtime ships) — permanently out of scope
  • Multi-node TCP/RDMA transport (production UCX/RDMA path beyond the in-proc/MPI stub) — PhaseDeferred("Phase 7")
  • Speculative decoding / continuous batching — permanently out of scope
  • Pipeline parallelism across transformer layers (out of this runtime's single-layer scope; caller's responsibility) — permanently out of scope
  • Kernel autotuning / tile-size search (incl. block-sparse GEMM tiling) — PhaseDeferred("Phase 5")

1.4 Intended Deployment Model

  • Library crate (moe-runtime): linked into a model executor or research harness.
  • Single-node multi-process EP: multiple OS processes each holding a rank's expert weights; coordinated via shared-memory or loopback transport (Phase 3/4).
  • Multi-node EP: MPI/UCX all-to-all (PhaseDeferred("Phase 7")).
  • Single-process emulation: world_size=1, all-to-all is a no-op; used for correctness tests.
  • Assumes host OS: Linux x86-64 (NUMA, huge-pages available); macOS allowed for CI unit tests.
  • CUDA device path: FFI to cuBLAS/cuBLASLt for grouped GEMM (Phase 2+); CPU BLAS (OpenBLAS/MKL) for Phase 0/1 tests.

1.5 Target Users

Role Interaction
ML systems engineers Embed MoeLayer::forward in a transformer executor
Distributed systems researchers Tune capacity factor, EP topology, overlap strategy
Runtime developers (this project) Extend crates, add transports, wire telemetry
Benchmark operators Run moe-bench for throughput/latency profiling
Correctness testers Run differential harness vs dense reference

1.6 Phase-0 Deliverable — Crates

Locked crates (all stubbed + typed; logic filled per phase):

  1. moe-core — core types: Tensor, ExpertId, TokenIdx, GateLogits, RoutingDecision, DispatchPlan, CombineBuffer, MoeError
  2. moe-gate — gating/router: dense top-k, softmax-over-topk, noisy top-k, expert-choice, aux load-balance + z-loss
  3. moe-dispatch — token→expert dispatch + combine: capacity buffers, permutation/scatter-gather, drop policy, weighted combine
  4. moe-expert — expert FFN module: expert-weight storage (sharded), batched/grouped GEMM orchestration (FFI), activation
  5. moe-comm — collectives: all-to-all, all-reduce, EP process-group mgmt; in-proc + multi-proc transports
  6. moe-sched — execution scheduler: streams/queues, comm/compute overlap, micro-batch pipelining, double-buffering
  7. moe-runtime — top-level MoE layer executor: orchestrates gate→dispatch→(a2a)→expert→(a2a)→combine; public MoeLayer::forward
  8. moe-telemetry — metrics (expert utilization, drop rate, routing entropy, comm/compute time), tracing spans
  9. moe-cli — driver: load config/weights, run layer, dump stats
  10. moe-bench — benchmarks + differential harness

1.7 Crate Capability Unlock Sequence

Coarse-grained capability milestones (M0…M10), each spanning one or more of the numbered phases in the Phase-by-Phase Plan (§12 onward); see that plan for the authoritative phase-by-phase breakdown.

Milestone Unlock Spans Phase(s)
M0 Workspace scaffold, all crates typed, moe-core complete, moe-gate dense top-k Phase 0
M1 moe-dispatch full capacity/drop, correctness tests pass Phase 1–3
M2 moe-expert CPU BLAS GEMM, moe-comm in-proc a2a, moe-runtime end-to-end single-rank forward pass Phase 5–6
M3 moe-sched overlap, double-buffer; multi-process EP (shared-mem transport) Phase 6, 8
M4 CUDA GEMM FFI, GPU tensor paths, moe-telemetry full metrics Phase 5, 12
M5 Multi-node EP (UCX/MPI stub), horizontal scaling tests Phase 7
M6 Checkpoint/weight-load hardening, moe-bench full suite Phase 11, 13
M7 Backward pass hooks (gradient w.r.t. inputs; aux-loss gradient) Phase 4 (z-loss grad), 6 (EP grad)
M8 Expert-choice routing, shared experts, advanced capacity strategies Phase 9
M9–M10 Int8/fp8 expert-weight quantization (built in Phase 10); INT4 and kernel autotuning remain deferred per §1.3 Phase 10

1.8 Timeline

  • Phase 0: 1–2 weeks (scaffold, types, gate CPU path)
  • Phases 1–2: 2–3 weeks (dispatch, expert GEMM, end-to-end)
  • Phases 3–4: 3–4 weeks (sched overlap, CUDA, telemetry)
  • Phase 5–6: 2–3 weeks (multi-node, checkpoint)
  • Phase 7–8: 2–4 weeks (backward, expert-choice)
  • Full implementation: 12–20 weeks total

1.9 Recommended Language + Rationale

Rust (edition 2021, MSRV 1.78)

  • No GC pauses → inference tail-latency determinism (p99 must not spike on GC)
  • Fearless concurrency → dispatch/combine + comm/compute overlap across threads/async
  • Explicit large-tensor lifecycle → ExpertWeights shards pinned in arena; no surprise allocs
  • Safe FFI → cuBLAS/OpenBLAS GEMM with unsafe isolated to FFI boundary modules
  • Zero-cost abstractions → RoutingDecision, DispatchPlan as stack types; no boxing hot path
  • Rejected C++: memory safety burden + portfolio consistency preference
  • Rejected Python: GIL + interpreter overhead incompatible with tight dispatch/overlap loops

1.10 Required Prerequisites

Domain Specific knowledge
Systems programming Memory layout, NUMA, huge-pages, cache lines, SIMD alignment
Concurrency Rust async/tokio, thread pools, lock-free queues, memory ordering
Distributed systems Process groups, collective operations, all-to-all semantics, rank assignment
ML systems Transformer architecture, MoE layer position, gating function math, capacity factor
Networking IPC (shared-memory, pipes), MPI/UCX concepts for Phase 5
Linear algebra GEMM shapes, grouped/batched GEMM, activation functions (SwiGLU, GELU)
FFI Rust extern "C", *mut f32 lifetime discipline, CUDA stream semantics

1.11 Success Criteria (Phase 0 Exit)

  • All 10 crates compile with cargo check (no warnings, #![deny(warnings)])
  • moe-core types pass round-trip serde + size assertions
  • moe-gate dense top-k correctness test (vs NumPy reference fixture)
  • FINAL_BLUEPRINT.md accepted by reviewer; no implementation code in blueprint

2. Why Build This

2.1 Unique Systems Knowledge Taught

Concept What you learn
Sparse routing Top-k selection under capacity constraints; load-balance vs quality tradeoff
Token dispatch/combine Permutation arithmetic, scatter-gather, drop policy with residual passthrough
Expert parallelism Rank-to-expert assignment, symmetric a2a buffer sizing, all-to-all correctness
Comm/compute overlap CUDA stream interleaving, double-buffering, dependency tracking across async stages
Capacity management Capacity factor math, overflow determinism, drop-rate vs quality curve
Grouped/batched GEMM Variable-length batch problem, padding vs packing strategies (Megablocks block-sparse)
Auxiliary losses Importance loss, load-balance loss, z-loss: how regularizers shape routing during training
Routing entropy Collapse detection, monitoring, recovery strategies

2.2 Historical + Architectural Significance

  • MoE scaling: Shazeer 2017 showed 137B parameter model at LSTM-quality cost → scaling law inflection
  • GShard / Switch Transformer: industrial-scale training (trillion parameters) via sparse activation
  • Mixtral 2023: brought MoE to open-weight inference → production relevance today
  • Architectural insight: MoE decouples parameter count from FLOPs per token — critical for efficient large models
  • This runtime embodies the execution engine beneath every modern sparse LLM inference stack

2.3 Engineering Muscles Developed

  • Correctness under permutation: permutation algebra bugs are silent (wrong output, no crash)
  • Performance cliff awareness: capacity factor too low → quality drops; too high → memory OOM
  • Distributed protocol design: all-to-all buffer layout must be symmetric; asymmetry causes hangs
  • FFI discipline: CUDA lifetime bugs crash only at runtime; Rust FFI boundary must be airtight
  • Telemetry-driven debugging: routing collapse, expert starvation, drop spikes — all invisible without metrics
  • Overlap scheduling: comm/compute overlap requires explicit dependency graphs; naive ordering leaves 40–60% GPU idle
  • Determinism under parallelism: bit-exact reproducibility requires tie-breaking rules + seeded noise

2.4 Production Engineering Concepts Exercised

Concept Exercise
Backpressure Capacity invariant enforcement; drop policy as natural backpressure
Observability Expert utilization, routing entropy, drop rate as first-class metrics
Graceful degradation Dropped tokens take residual passthrough — no crash, bounded quality loss
Idempotency DispatchPlan is deterministic; re-execution produces same routing
Zero-downtime reconfiguration PhaseDeferred("Phase 6") — hot expert weight reload / live EP resize
Resource budgeting Arena allocator for expert-weight tensors; no unbounded heap growth
Failure isolation MoeError typed variants; partial expert failure returns error, not panic
Operational runbooks moe-cli stats dump + telemetry dashboard spec in §§ Operational Readiness

2.5 Cross-Links to Portfolio Projects

Portfolio project Intersection
GPU/Tensor Runtime Tensor handle design, CUDA stream mgmt, FFI GEMM patterns
Distributed KV-store / Consensus Process-group topology, all-to-all as collective; rank assignment
Execution Scheduler / Task DAG moe-sched overlap DAG; micro-batch pipelining
Network Stack / IPC moe-comm transport layer; shared-mem ring, UCX stub
Compiler / IR Routing decision as dataflow; dispatch plan as lowered IR
Vector DB Similarity-based expert routing analogy; HNSW vs top-k gating
Storage Engine Arena allocator for expert weights; mmap for weight shards
HTTP Server / Load Balancer Token dispatch as request routing; capacity factor as queue depth

2.6 Calibration Value

  • Depth signal: routing math + all-to-all + GEMM FFI + overlap scheduling = 4 hard subsystems in one project
  • Breadth signal: touches distributed systems, ML math, CUDA FFI, Rust concurrency simultaneously
  • Differentiator: few engineers have implemented MoE dispatch/combine from scratch; most use framework black boxes
  • Interview coverage: system design (EP topology), correctness (capacity/drop invariants), performance (overlap), debugging (routing collapse)
  • Research relevance: enables experimentation with custom routing strategies unavailable in locked frameworks

2.7 Why Not Use Existing Frameworks

Framework Gap
DeepSpeed-MoE Python/CUDA; opaque C++ extensions; no Rust integration
Tutel Python-first; adaptive parallelism hidden in CUDA ops
Megablocks Block-sparse CUDA kernel; no orchestration layer
fairscale MoE PyTorch-dependent; no standalone runtime
Megatron-LM Monolithic; impossible to embed as library
Building from scratch forces understanding every protocol decision. FFI consumer of GEMM kernels keeps scope tight while retaining integration realism.

2.8 Difficulty + Time Investment Justification

  • Phase 0–2 (2–5 weeks): hard enough to demonstrate competence; soft enough for a portfolio sprint
  • Phases 3–5 (overlap + multi-node): distinguish senior from staff; most candidates stop here
  • Phase 7–8 (backward + expert-choice): research-grade additions; optional depth signal
  • Full completion (12–20 weeks): principal-level signal; comparable depth to a research internship deliverable

2.9 What Mastery Looks Like

A practitioner who has fully internalized this project can do the following without prompting or reference material:

  • Derive the capacity formula from first principles (Phase 2): given n_tokens, k, E, and capacity_factor, compute the exact per-expert buffer size and predict drop rate at any load distribution; explain why the ceil is load-bearing for correctness.
  • Reconstruct the all-to-all protocol from scratch (Phase 5): specify send-counts, recv-counts, and buffer layout for an arbitrary EP world size; identify the symmetry invariant that prevents deadlock; design a test that would catch an off-by-one in count computation.
  • Trace a routing collapse (Phase 6 + §11 Failure Modes): given a telemetry dump showing one expert receiving 80%+ of tokens, identify whether the cause is gate logit collapse, capacity misconfiguration, or aux-loss coefficient too low, and state the correct remediation for each.
  • Prove token conservation (Phase 3 + I1): walk through the scatter → expert compute → gather → combine → residual-passthrough code path and demonstrate that every TokenIdx in the input batch appears exactly once in the output tensor regardless of capacity_factor.
  • Size the VRAM budget (Phase 4 + §4): given a model with 64 experts, d_model=4096, d_ffn=16384, BF16, EP=8, compute the per-rank expert weight footprint, activations peak, and combined-buffer size; state which term dominates and why.
  • Design the comm/compute overlap schedule (Phase 8 + ADR-05): draw the double-buffer state machine, label the CUDA events, and explain why the overlap liveness invariant (I6) prevents a deadlock when the compute stream finishes before the comm stream.
  • Write the ADR for a new routing variant (any Phase): given expert-choice routing, fill all six ADR columns including the "When the Rejected Choice Wins" column with a concrete token-budget regime where the rejected token-choice approach outperforms.

3. Architectural Overview

3.1 Canonical Architecture Diagram

    input tokens [n_tokens, d_model]
            |
      [moe-gate] router W_g.x -> logits -> top-k -> softmax weights; noisy/expert-choice; aux + z-loss
            |  RoutingDecision (expert ids + weights)
      [moe-dispatch] capacity check/drop -> build permutation -> scatter tokens into per-expert contiguous buffers
            |  per-expert token batches
      [moe-comm] all-to-all across EP ranks   <->   [moe-sched] overlap a2a with expert GEMM (streams, double-buffer)
            |
      [moe-expert] each expert = FFN: GEMM(w_in) -> activation -> GEMM(w_out); batched/grouped; weights sharded per rank
            |  expert outputs
      [moe-comm] all-to-all back
            |
      [moe-dispatch] combine: un-permute -> gate-weighted sum; dropped tokens take residual passthrough
            |  output tokens [n_tokens, d_model]
      [moe-runtime] orchestrates the whole layer; [moe-telemetry] taps every stage

3.2 Data Flow Summary

Every stage is a pure function over typed buffers. No shared mutable global state between stages. Each arrow is a typed Rust struct crossing a crate boundary.

GateLogits -> RoutingDecision -> DispatchPlan -> [per-expert Tensor batches]
-> [a2a out-buffer] -> [expert output Tensors] -> [a2a in-buffer]
-> CombineBuffer -> output Tensor

3.3 moe-gate — Gating / Router

  • Responsibility: compute per-token expert scores; select top-k; produce RoutingDecision
  • Inputs: input: Tensor[n_tokens, d_model], W_g: Tensor[d_model, num_experts]
  • Outputs: GateLogits[n_tokens, num_experts] (pre-softmax); RoutingDecision[n_tokens]
  • Operations: linear projection → optional Gaussian noise injection (noisy top-k) → top-k selection → softmax over selected → expert-choice variant (transpose: expert selects tokens) → aux importance loss + z-loss accumulation into AuxLossState
  • Concurrency boundary: CPU-side pure compute; embarrassingly parallel over token dimension; no shared state
  • Failure modes: NaN in logits → MoeError::NanInLogits; all experts over capacity → all tokens dropped (catastrophic — monitored)
  • Invariant: sum of gate weights over kept experts = 1.0 per token (softmax renormalization after drop)
  • Rejected alternative: argmax routing (top-1) — supported as k=1 special case; not a separate code path

3.4 moe-dispatch — Token Dispatch + Combine

  • Responsibility: enforce capacity invariant; build DispatchPlan; scatter tokens into per-expert contiguous buffers; on return path, un-permute and gate-weight-sum
  • Inputs: RoutingDecision, input: Tensor[n_tokens, d_model], capacity: Capacity
  • Outputs (dispatch): DispatchPlan (permutation + per_expert_counts + dropped bitset); per-expert Tensor views
  • Outputs (combine): output: Tensor[n_tokens, d_model]
  • Capacity math: Capacity = ceil(capacity_factor * n_tokens * k / num_experts); tokens exceeding per-expert buffer dropped deterministically (lowest gate weight first, tie-break by TokenIdx)
  • Scatter: perm[i] = position of token i in the permuted buffer; contiguous per-expert sub-slices
  • Dropped token handling: residual passthrough — output[dropped_token] = input[dropped_token]; no zero-fill
  • Concurrency boundary: permutation build is sequential (O(n_tokens * k)); scatter/gather parallelizable via Rayon
  • Failure modes: perm length mismatch → panic in debug, MoeError in release; capacity=0 → all dropped

3.5 moe-comm — Collective Communication

  • Responsibility: all-to-all token exchange across EP ranks (pre-expert and post-expert); all-reduce for aux losses
  • Transport variants:
    • InProc: Arc<Mutex<Vec<u8>>> ring per rank pair (single-process EP emulation)
    • SharedMem: posix shm segments (multi-process, same node, Phase 3)
    • Ucx stub: PhaseDeferred("Phase 7")
  • all-to-all semantics: each rank sends send_counts[r] tokens to rank r; receives recv_counts[r] tokens from rank r; counts must be symmetric (EP consistency invariant)
  • Buffer layout: [rank_0_tokens | rank_1_tokens | ... | rank_{W-1}_tokens] contiguous; offsets from per_expert_counts
  • EpGroup: { rank: u32, world_size: u32, transport: Transport }; initialized once at MoeLayer construction
  • Failure modes: asymmetric counts → MoeError::A2aCountMismatch; transport timeout → MoeError::CommTimeout; deadlock on barrier if any rank crashes — detected via watchdog (Phase 5)
  • Protocol boundary: transport trait object; send / recv / barrier as async methods

3.6 moe-sched — Execution Scheduler

  • Responsibility: schedule comm/compute overlap via streams; double-buffer micro-batches; track inter-stage dependencies
  • Core abstraction: Stream (CUDA stream or CPU thread pool queue); WorkItem enum (CommOp, GemmOp, CombineOp)
  • Overlap strategy: while all-to-all for micro-batch N is in-flight, execute GEMM for micro-batch N-1 on separate stream; requires double-buffer (two CombineBuffer slots)
  • Dependency tracking: DAG of WorkItem nodes; edges enforce: a2a-complete before GEMM-start; GEMM-complete before combine-start
  • Double-buffer invariant: buffer slot alternates per micro-batch index; never read and write same slot concurrently
  • Micro-batch pipelining: split n_tokens into P micro-batches; pipeline depth = 2 (can extend to 3, PhaseDeferred("Phase 8"))
  • Failure modes: stream sync timeout → MoeError::StreamTimeout; DAG cycle (bug) → detected at construction, not runtime
  • Concurrency boundary: scheduler thread owns stream handles; worker threads execute ops; channels for completion signals

3.7 moe-expert — Expert FFN Module

  • Responsibility: execute per-expert FFN for a batch of tokens assigned to that expert; manage expert weight shards
  • FFN structure: GEMM(w_in: [d_model, d_ff]) -> activation -> GEMM(w_out: [d_ff, d_model])
  • Weight storage: ExpertWeights { w_in: Tensor, w_out: Tensor }; sharded across EP ranks: expert e lives on rank e % world_size; each rank stores only its shard
  • GEMM dispatch: CPU path → OpenBLAS sgemm via FFI; GPU path → cuBLASLt grouped GEMM via FFI (Phase 2+)
  • Batched/grouped GEMM: variable token counts per expert → padding to power-of-2 or Megablocks block-sparse strategy (PhaseDeferred("Phase 5"); see Alternative Architectures Alt-1)
  • Activation: SwiGLU (default), GELU, ReLU — selected at MoeLayer config; applied element-wise between GEMMs
  • Concurrency boundary: each expert is independent; experts on same rank can execute in parallel (thread pool)
  • Failure modes: zero tokens assigned → skip GEMM, return empty Tensor; weight shape mismatch → MoeError::ShapeMismatch

3.8 moe-runtime — Layer Orchestrator

  • Responsibility: single public API MoeLayer::forward(input, aux_state) -> Result<Tensor, MoeError>; owns all sub-components; orchestrates gate→dispatch→a2a→expert→a2a→combine sequence
  • Owns: Gate, Dispatcher, EpGroup, Scheduler, ExpertShards, Telemetry
  • Construction: MoeLayer::new(config: MoeConfig, weights: ExpertWeights, ep_group: EpGroup) -> Self
  • Forward pass contract: stateless w.r.t. token data; AuxLossState accumulates across forward calls; reset explicitly
  • Error propagation: ? operator; any sub-error surfaces as MoeError variant; no panics in release build
  • Trust boundary: caller supplies input: Tensor; runtime validates shape, dtype, device before any computation
  • Concurrency model: MoeLayer is Send + Sync; multiple threads may call forward on different micro-batches; internal scheduler serializes stream ops

3.9 moe-telemetry — Observability

  • Responsibility: capture per-forward-pass metrics; expose Prometheus-compatible counters + histograms; emit tracing spans
  • Metrics: expert utilization (tokens/expert / capacity), drop rate (dropped/total), routing entropy (Shannon entropy over gate weights), a2a latency (µs), GEMM latency (µs/expert), combine latency (µs)
  • Tracing spans: one root span per forward call; child spans per stage (gate, dispatch, a2a-out, expert, a2a-in, combine)
  • Tap points: telemetry receives typed events via channel from each crate; zero-copy (pass references, not clones)
  • Failure modes: telemetry channel full → drop metric (non-blocking); never block forward pass for metrics

3.10 moe-core — Shared Types

  • Responsibility: define all cross-crate types; no logic except constructors + invariant assertions
  • Arena: bump allocator for Tensor storage; owned by MoeLayer; freed at layer teardown
  • MoeError: typed enum covering all failure variants; implements std::error::Error; no string-only errors
  • Layout: { strides: SmallVec<[usize; 6]>, offset: usize }; supports non-contiguous tensors for combine views

3.11 Concurrency + Persistence + Protocol Boundaries Summary

Boundary Type Notes
gate → dispatch In-process typed struct RoutingDecision stack-allocated for small k
dispatch → comm In-process buffer DispatchPlan + permuted Tensor views
comm → expert Transport (trait object) a2a buffer; async send/recv
expert → comm In-process buffer expert output Tensors
sched → workers MPSC channel WorkItem enqueue
telemetry taps MPSC channel Non-blocking send
Persistence None (Phase 0–4) Weight shards mmap in Phase 6
External interface MoeLayer::forward Only public API; all else crate-internal

4. System Constraints

4.1 Constraints Table

Dimension Constraint Notes
Model scale num_experts: 8–64; d_model: 1024–8192; d_ff: 4096–32768 Config-driven; runtime validated at construction
Batch size n_tokens: 1–32768 per forward call Micro-batch split if > sched.max_micro_batch_tokens
Expert parallelism EP world_size: 1–16 ranks (Phase 3); up to 128 (Phase 5) Symmetric: num_experts divisible by world_size
Top-k k: 1–8 (typical 2–4) k > 8: performance degrades; gate matrix dominates
Capacity factor 1.0–2.0 (f32); typical 1.25 < 1.0: invariant violation; > 4.0: memory OOM risk
Peak memory per rank ≤ GPU VRAM or 128 GB host RAM Expert weights dominate; arena must fit shard
Expert weight shard size num_experts/world_size × (w_in + w_out) × dtype_bytes e.g. 8 experts/rank × 2 × 8192 × 32768 × 4B = ~17 GB (FP32)
Throughput target ≥ 80% of dense FFN throughput at capacity_factor=1.25, k=2 Measured by moe-bench; drop to 60% acceptable at k=4
p50 forward latency ≤ 1.2× dense FFN (same d_model, d_ff) Single-rank, warm cache
p95 forward latency ≤ 1.5× dense FFN Includes occasional capacity-overflow drop path
p99 forward latency ≤ 2.0× dense FFN GC-pause-free (Rust); p99 spike budget = 0 ms GC
a2a latency (in-proc) ≤ 50 µs (world_size=4, 4096 tokens) SharedMem transport target
a2a latency (multi-node) ≤ 500 µs (world_size=8, 4096 tokens, 25 Gbps NIC) Phase 5 UCX target
Startup time ≤ 5 s (weight load from disk, world_size=1) mmap path (Phase 6); initial load may be longer
Shutdown / teardown ≤ 100 ms Drain in-flight ops; release arena; close EP group
Max concurrency ≤ 16 concurrent forward calls per MoeLayer Scheduler queue depth; beyond this → backpressure
Thread pool size CPU: num_cpus; GPU: 1 CUDA stream per expert group Configurable via MoeConfig.thread_pool_size
NUMA Expert weight tensors allocated on NUMA node local to GPU mmap + mbind advisory (Phase 4)
Huge pages 2 MB huge pages for expert weight arenas ≥ 1 GB MAP_HUGETLB advisory; graceful fallback
File descriptor budget ≤ 64 fds per rank (shm segments + log files) Validated at startup
Network RTT assumption ≤ 1 ms intra-node (loopback); ≤ 5 ms intra-cluster Phase 5 multi-node baseline
Availability target N/A (library; no daemon) Caller's availability model applies
Failure budget Correctness: 0 silent wrong outputs; drop rate ≤ 5% at capacity_factor=1.25 Monitored via moe-telemetry
RTO N/A for library; MoeError returned in ≤ 10 ms on any transport failure Caller decides retry/fallback
Storage amplification Expert weights stored once per rank shard; no duplication Phase 6: weight file = sum of all rank shards
IO amplification mmap: page faults on cold load; steady-state = 0 IO Hot-path: all weights in DRAM/VRAM

4.2 Memory Sizing Formula

shard_bytes = (num_experts / world_size) * (d_model * d_ff + d_ff * d_model) * dtype_bytes
capacity_buffer_bytes = capacity * d_model * dtype_bytes * num_experts_per_rank
total_per_rank = shard_bytes + 2 * capacity_buffer_bytes  (double-buffer)
  • dtype_bytes: FP32=4, BF16=2; BF16 halves shard size but requires FP32 accumulator (FFI contract)
  • Constraint: total_per_rank ≤ 0.85 * available_memory (15% headroom for activations, stack, OS)

4.3 Throughput + Latency Model

Stage Dominant cost Scaling
Gate (CPU) Linear projection: O(n_tokens * d_model * num_experts) Parallelizes over tokens
Dispatch Permutation build: O(n_tokens * k) Sequential; < 1 ms for n_tokens ≤ 8192
a2a Bandwidth: (n_tokens * d_model * dtype_bytes) per direction Scales with world_size
Expert GEMM O(tokens_per_expert * d_model * d_ff) Dominant; grouped GEMM amortizes
Combine O(n_tokens * k * d_model) Parallelizes over tokens
Overlap gain Up to 40–60% wall-time reduction Only if GEMM ≥ a2a time

4.4 Scaling Assumptions

  • Vertical: larger d_model/d_ff → proportional GEMM cost; capacity buffer grows linearly
  • Horizontal (EP): adding ranks reduces per-rank GEMM work by world_size; a2a cost grows; crossover point ~8 ranks for typical d_ff=16384
  • Batch scaling: larger n_tokens → better GEMM utilization; dispatch permutation remains O(n_tokens * k)
  • Expert count scaling: more experts → lower utilization per expert (capacity invariant tightens); routing entropy increases; aux-loss weight must increase proportionally

4.5 Platform Constraints

Constraint Value
OS Linux x86-64 (primary); macOS arm64 (CI only, no CUDA)
MSRV Rust 1.78 (edition 2021)
CUDA ≥ 11.8 for cuBLASLt grouped GEMM (Phase 2+)
OpenBLAS ≥ 0.3.23 for CPU GEMM (Phase 0/1 CI)
MPI OpenMPI ≥ 4.1 or MPICH ≥ 4.0 (Phase 5 only)
Kernel Linux ≥ 5.15 (io_uring, huge-page improvements)
libc glibc ≥ 2.31

6. Design Principles

6.1 Token Conservation Is Inviolable

Every input token either (a) routes to exactly K experts and recombines with normalized gate weights summing to 1.0 over kept assignments, or (b) is explicitly dropped and passes through residual unchanged. No silent loss, no double-counting.

  • Tradeoff: enforcing the invariant requires a BitSet of dropped tokens and a residual passthrough path — adds ~8 bytes/token bookkeeping.
  • Operational: monitoring tokens_dropped_total counter alerts when capacity is too tight; non-zero residual passthrough is expected, not an error.
  • Violation consequence: output tensor shape matches input but values are wrong; model loss diverges silently; only differential test against dense reference catches it.

6.2 Deterministic Routing Under Identical Inputs

Given the same (input, seed, config), moe-gate and moe-dispatch must produce bit-identical RoutingDecision and DispatchPlan. Tie-break by ascending ExpertId. Noisy top-k uses seeded PRNG derived from (batch_id, layer_id, token_idx).

  • Tradeoff: non-determinism would simplify load-balancing heuristics but makes debugging and differential testing impossible.
  • Operational: run determinism smoke test on every CI push; log routing_entropy — sudden entropy collapse signals PRNG reuse bug.
  • Violation consequence: two identical batches route differently; A/B correctness comparison breaks; distributed checksum mismatch across EP ranks.

6.3 Bounded Resource Usage — No Unbounded Allocation on Hot Path

Every buffer on the forward/backward hot path is pre-allocated or drawn from an arena. DispatchPlan, CombineBuffer, per-expert token buffers: all sized at layer-init time to Capacity = ceil(capacity_factor * n_tokens * k / num_experts).

  • Tradeoff: requires knowing n_tokens max at init; inference batches of variable size pad or slice to declared max.
  • Operational: arena high-water-mark metric; OOM at init (not during inference) is the desired failure mode.
  • Violation consequence: Vec::push inside forward pass causes GC-equivalent pause spikes; tail P99 latency explodes.

6.4 Observability by Default — Every Stage Emits Spans and Metrics

moe-telemetry is not optional infrastructure. Every crate calls into it unconditionally; the backend is a zero-overhead no-op when telemetry is disabled (compile-time feature gate).

  • Mandatory metrics: expert utilization histogram, drop rate per expert, routing entropy, per-stage wall-clock, comm/compute overlap ratio, GEMM TFLOPS.
  • Tradeoff: span creation has ~20 ns overhead per call; acceptable vs. stage latencies of O(ms).
  • Operational: telemetry data is the primary debugging tool for load imbalance, comm bottlenecks, and capacity misconfiguration.
  • Violation consequence: without utilization metrics, capacity factor tuning is guesswork; load imbalance bugs survive to production.

6.5 Explicit Backpressure — Never Block Silently

moe-sched stream queues are bounded. When a producer enqueues faster than a consumer drains, it receives Err(MoeError::QueueFull) — never a silent block. Caller decides: drop micro-batch, shed load, or propagate.

  • Tradeoff: requires explicit error handling at every enqueue site; simpler designs block and hide backpressure.
  • Operational: queue_depth gauge; alert on sustained queue_depth > 80 % capacity.
  • Violation consequence: silent blocking in dispatch saturates CUDA streams; all-to-all stalls; entire pipeline deadlocks.

6.6 Fail Fast and Loud — No Silent Degradation

Invariant violations (capacity overflow, shape mismatch, rank asymmetry, NaN in gate logits) must immediately return Err(MoeError::*) with structured context. Panics only for programmer errors (index out of bounds in debug mode); debug_assert! guards on hot path.

  • Tradeoff: recoverable errors require Result plumbing everywhere; heavier API surface.
  • Operational: structured error context (layer_id, token_idx, expert_id) enables immediate root-cause isolation.
  • Violation consequence: silent NaN in gate weights produces zero-weighted combine outputs; loss appears normal; silent model degradation for many iterations.

6.7 FFI Safety at Every Boundary

All GEMM backend calls (cublas, cutlass, external BLAS) cross an explicit FFI boundary with lifetime-annotated raw pointer wrappers. Safety invariants documented on every unsafe block. No unsafe outside moe-expert and moe-comm transport internals.

  • Tradeoff: wrapper types add indirection; justified by preventing UAF on expert weight buffers during concurrent GEMM.
  • Operational: AddressSanitizer + Valgrind run on FFI boundary tests in CI.
  • Violation consequence: use-after-free of expert weight shards during EP reshuffle; silent data corruption with no Rust borrow-checker protection.

6.8 Communication/Compute Overlap Is Explicit, Not Incidental

moe-sched double-buffers dispatch and combine all-to-all operations against expert GEMM. The overlap is a first-class architectural concern with explicit stream assignments, not an emergent property of async fire-and-forget.

  • Tradeoff: double-buffering doubles CombineBuffer memory; worthwhile when comm latency ≥ 30 % of GEMM latency.
  • Operational: overlap_efficiency metric = (comm_time_hidden / total_comm_time); target ≥ 0.7 at batch_size ≥ 512.
  • Violation consequence: serialized comm+compute yields 1.3–2× slower MoE layer vs. dense equivalent; defeats the sparsity advantage.

6.9 EP Consistency — Expert Ownership Is Deterministic

Expert e always lives on rank e % world_size. This mapping is fixed at layer init and never re-derived per-token. All-to-all routing depends on it; any deviation corrupts token→expert assignment.

  • Tradeoff: non-power-of-two expert counts produce uneven load; handled by capacity factor, not by dynamic remapping.
  • Operational: on init, all ranks log their expert ownership list; checksum verified across group.
  • Violation consequence: token sent to wrong rank; expert computes garbage input; output recombined with wrong weights; numerically plausible but semantically wrong.

6.10 Correctness Before Optimization

Dense-reference equivalence test runs on every changed function in moe-gate, moe-dispatch, moe-expert. No performance optimization ships without the differential harness passing at capacity=inf, k=num_experts.

  • Tradeoff: doubles test time for numerically sensitive paths.
  • Operational: CI gate: moe-bench --differential must pass before merge.
  • Violation consequence: optimized grouped GEMM produces wrong expert output; only caught by dense reference diff, not by loss curve (which degrades slowly).

6.11 Shape-First API Design

Every public function signature encodes tensor shapes and constraints in types, not documentation. GateLogits carries [n_tokens, num_experts]; RoutingDecision carries K. Mismatched shapes are type errors, not runtime panics.

  • Tradeoff: richer type system requires const-generic or newtype boilerplate.
  • Operational: shape mismatches caught at compile time during integration of moe-gate → moe-dispatch pipeline.
  • Violation consequence: dynamic shape mismatch surfaces at layer boundary; hard to attribute to gate vs. dispatch without shape-typed API.

6.12 Configuration Is Code — No Magic Numbers Inline

All tunable parameters (capacity_factor, k, num_experts, EP world_size, queue depth, noise std-dev) come from MoeConfig struct, validated at layer init. No const literals in hot-path logic.

  • Tradeoff: config validation code at init path; small cost.
  • Operational: config dump in structured log at startup; diff-able across runs.
  • Violation consequence: hard-coded capacity factor survives a config change; ops can't tune without recompile.

6.13 Single Writer Per Buffer

Each dispatch buffer, combine buffer, and expert-weight shard has exactly one writer at any time. Double-buffering in moe-sched achieves overlap by alternating ownership, not concurrent write.

  • Tradeoff: requires handoff protocol (semaphore or atomic fence) at buffer swap boundary.
  • Operational: data-race detector (TSAN equivalent via Loom) validates handoff correctness in moe-sched unit tests.
  • Violation consequence: concurrent writes to combine buffer; non-deterministic output; only Loom / TSAN detects it.

6.14 Graceful Degradation Under Expert Failure

If a remote expert rank is unreachable during all-to-all, affected tokens are dropped and marked as residual passthrough rather than stalling the pipeline. The failure is recorded in MoeError::ExpertUnavailable with rank and expert IDs.

  • Tradeoff: model quality degrades proportionally; better than full pipeline stall.
  • Operational: expert_unavailable_total{rank, expert_id} counter; alert threshold configurable.
  • Violation consequence: blocking on dead rank stalls entire batch; cascading timeout across all EP peers.

6.15 Zero-Copy Weight Access on Hot Path

Expert weights are mmap-backed (SafeTensors layout) and accessed via ExpertWeights handle without deserialization copy on the forward path. Copy happens once at load time into device (GPU/CPU) memory.

  • Tradeoff: mmap-backed format must match device dtype; conversion cost at load acceptable.
  • Operational: weight load latency logged; unexpected re-copy during forward (e.g., host fallback) fires weight_copy_unexpected metric.
  • Violation consequence: per-forward weight copy saturates PCIe/NVLink; GEMM is not the bottleneck anymore.

7. System Invariants

7.1 Token Conservation

Statement: |output_tokens| == |input_tokens|; for every non-dropped token t: output[t] = Σ_{(e,w) ∈ RoutingDecision[t]} w * expert_e(token_t); for every dropped token t: output[t] = input[t] (residual passthrough). Gate weights over kept assignments sum to 1.0 within ±1e-5.

Attribute Detail
Why it matters Shape contract for stacking transformer layers; wrong output breaks all downstream layers
Where enforced moe-dispatch::combine() — un-permute step; residual passthrough applied from BitSet
Detection Differential test vs. dense-reference at capacity=inf; sum(gate_weights[t]) ∈ [0.999, 1.001] asserted post-softmax
Telemetry tokens_dropped_total{layer,expert} counter; gate_weight_sum_histogram
Recovery Drop detection is deterministic; retry with larger capacity_factor surfaced via MoeError::CapacityExceeded
Debugging Dump DispatchPlan.dropped BitSet + RoutingDecision for failing batch; compare combine output vs. dense reference element-wise

7.2 Capacity Invariant

Statement: per_expert_token_count[e] ≤ Capacity for all e ∈ [0, num_experts) after moe-dispatch::build_plan(). Capacity = ceil(capacity_factor * n_tokens * k / num_experts).

Attribute Detail
Why it matters GEMM buffer pre-allocation depends on exact Capacity; overflow → buffer OOB write
Where enforced moe-dispatch::build_plan() — deterministic drop: sort overflow tokens ascending by gate weight, drop lowest
Detection debug_assert!(count ≤ capacity) in dispatch; unit tests with capacity=1 trigger forced drops
Telemetry expert_buffer_utilization{expert_id} gauge (count/capacity); capacity_overflow_events_total
Recovery None at runtime — drop is the recovery; capacity_factor increase is an offline config change
Debugging Log (token_idx, expert_id, gate_weight) for every dropped token; histogram of drop depth

7.3 Routing Determinism

Statement: For fixed (input_tensor, seed, MoeConfig), moe-gate and moe-dispatch produce identical RoutingDecision and DispatchPlan across repeated calls, processes, and restarts. Tie-breaking: ascending ExpertId.

Attribute Detail
Why it matters Reproducibility for debugging, differential testing, and gradient checkpointing
Where enforced moe-gate: softmax reduction order fixed (left-to-right); PRNG seeded per (batch_id, layer_id, token_idx); tie-break in topk_indices()
Detection Hash RoutingDecision + DispatchPlan; compare two identical forward passes; must be bit-identical
Telemetry routing_entropy_bits{layer} histogram; entropy collapse (< 0.5 bits) signals degenerate routing
Recovery Non-determinism is a code bug, not a runtime error; gate behind CI determinism smoke test
Debugging Log PRNG state at gate entry; replay with fixed seed; compare topk index arrays element-wise

7.4 EP Ownership Consistency

Statement: Expert e resides on rank e % world_size. The mapping is computed once at EpGroup::init() and is read-only for the lifetime of the layer. All-to-all send/recv counts computed from this mapping must be symmetric: send_count[r] == recv_count_at_rank_r.

Attribute Detail
Why it matters Token routed to wrong rank silently corrupts expert output
Where enforced moe-comm::dispatch_a2a() — verifies send_count matches per-rank token assignments; EpGroup ownership map validated at init
Detection Rank-level checksum of received token indices after a2a; mismatches raise MoeError::RankMismatch
Telemetry a2a_send_recv_count_mismatch_total{rank} counter
Recovery Fatal error; pipeline cannot continue with corrupted routing; restart required
Debugging Dump DispatchPlan.per_expert_counts at every rank; compare send vs. received counts; identify asymmetric rank

7.5 Combine Correctness

Statement: moe-dispatch::combine() output equals Σ_k gate_weight_k * expert_output_k for every non-dropped token; numerically matches dense-reference within atol=1e-3, rtol=1e-3 (fp32 accumulation order fixed).

Attribute Detail
Why it matters Wrong combine produces plausible but incorrect outputs — silent model quality degradation
Where enforced moe-dispatch::combine(): weighted sum in canonical permutation order; dropped tokens take residual
Detection moe-bench --differential at capacity=inf, k=num_experts; element-wise L∞ norm < 1e-3
Telemetry combine_l_inf_error{layer} histogram (sampled in test mode); dropped_token_residual_passthrough_total
Recovery Detected in CI; not a runtime-recoverable error
Debugging Dump per-expert output before combine; verify gate weights; compare partial sums vs. reference

7.6 Comm/Compute Overlap Liveness

Statement: moe-sched must not deadlock: every all-to-all eventually completes, every GEMM stream eventually drains. Double-buffer swap must occur within timeout_ms (configurable, default 5000 ms).

Attribute Detail
Why it matters Deadlock halts entire inference pipeline; no other layer can proceed
Where enforced moe-sched::step(): watchdog timer on stream drain; MoeError::StreamTimeout on expiry
Detection Watchdog thread monitors stream completion; fires stream_timeout_total counter
Telemetry stream_drain_latency_ms{stream_id} histogram; double_buffer_swap_latency_ms
Recovery On timeout: log state, drain all streams, return Err(MoeError::StreamTimeout); caller decides retry vs. abort
Debugging Dump stream queue depth at timeout; identify which stage (a2a or GEMM) is stalled; check NCCL rank health

7.7 Weight Shard Completeness

Statement: At MoeLayer::init(), every expert e assigned to the local rank has all required weight tensors loaded: ExpertWeights { w_in: [d_model, d_ff], w_out: [d_ff, d_model] }. No partial shard permitted.

Attribute Detail
Why it matters Partial weights produce incorrect GEMM output without error signal
Where enforced moe-expert::load_weights(): validates shape and dtype of every shard; returns Err(MoeError::WeightShardIncomplete) on mismatch
Detection Shape + checksum validation at load; debug_assert!(w_in.shape == [d_model, d_ff])
Telemetry weight_load_latency_ms{expert_id}, weight_shard_checksum_mismatch_total
Recovery Fatal at init; fail-fast before any forward pass
Debugging Log expected vs. actual shape + dtype + byte count for failing shard; check SafeTensors header

7.8 Aux Loss Accumulation Monotonicity

Statement: AuxLossState accumulates non-negative scalar values per forward pass. Values are reset exactly once per optimizer step via AuxLossState::reset(). No double-accumulation; no accumulation without a subsequent reset.

Attribute Detail
Why it matters Double-accumulated aux loss biases expert utilization penalty; over-regularizes routing
Where enforced moe-gate::accumulate_aux() — guarded by step-count token; reset() must be called before next step
Detection Assert step_token == expected in accumulate; unit test: two forward passes without reset → error
Telemetry aux_loss_value{layer}, z_loss_value{layer} scalar metrics per step
Recovery MoeError::AuxLossDoubleAccumulation on detection; caller must call reset()
Debugging Log step_token at each accumulate call; compare step_token sequence across layers

7.9 Gate Logit Numerical Safety

Statement: Gate logits must be finite and within [-logit_clamp, +logit_clamp] (default 30.0) before softmax. NaN or Inf in logits propagates to weights and silently zeros or NaNs combine output.

Attribute Detail
Why it matters NaN gate weights zero-out all expert outputs silently; model loss looks zero (not diverge)
Where enforced moe-gate::forward(): clamp + NaN check post-projection; return Err(MoeError::NanInGateLogits)
Detection logit.is_finite() check; unit test with NaN input → error
Telemetry gate_logit_clamp_events_total{layer}, gate_logit_nan_total{layer}
Recovery Return error immediately; do not propagate NaN downstream
Debugging Log (token_idx, expert_id, logit_value) for first N non-finite logits; check upstream projection layer for overflow

7.10 All-to-All Symmetric Count Protocol

Statement: For each pair of ranks (i, j) in EpGroup, send_count[i→j] == recv_count[j←i]. This must hold before any ncclAllToAll / MPI_Alltoallv call.

Attribute Detail
Why it matters NCCL/MPI AllToAll with asymmetric counts hangs or silently truncates data
Where enforced moe-comm::verify_symmetric_counts() called before every a2a; aborts with MoeError::AsymmetricA2ACounts
Detection Pre-condition check O(world_size²) integers — negligible vs. comm latency
Telemetry a2a_count_verify_failures_total{layer,direction}
Recovery Fatal: asymmetric counts indicate routing bug; must not proceed
Debugging Dump send_counts[rank] matrix at failure; compare DispatchPlan.per_expert_counts vs. expected per-rank totals

7.11 Single-Rank Short-Circuit Correctness

Statement: When EpGroup.world_size == 1, all-to-all is a no-op and tokens remain local. Dispatch and combine must produce identical results to the multi-rank path (verified by differential test).

Attribute Detail
Why it matters Single-rank mode is the primary development/test configuration; bugs here hide until distributed run
Where enforced moe-comm::maybe_a2a(): early return on world_size == 1 with no-copy token reuse
Detection Differential test: single-rank output == multi-rank output (same EP group, world_size=1 vs. 2)
Telemetry ep_world_size{layer} gauge; a2a_noop_total counter
Recovery Correctness bug; fix in code
Debugging Run with RUST_LOG=trace moe-cli --world-size 1; compare dispatch output tensor checksums

7.12 Backward-Pass Gradient Validity

Statement: Gradients flowing through moe-dispatch (scatter/gather) and moe-gate (softmax + top-k) must be non-NaN, non-Inf, and consistent with the forward-pass primal values for the kept experts. Dropped tokens contribute zero gradient.

Attribute Detail
Why it matters NaN gradients corrupt optimizer state; silent if loss scalar is NaN-masked
Where enforced moe-dispatch::combine_backward() — zero grad for dropped tokens; moe-gate::backward() — straight-through or softmax gradient depending on mode
Detection Gradient norm check after backward; grad.is_finite() assertion
Telemetry grad_norm{layer} histogram; nan_grad_events_total{layer}
Recovery Return Err(MoeError::NanGradient); abort training step
Debugging Bisect: check grad after combine, before combine, after GEMM — isolate which stage introduces NaN

8. Domain Model — MoE Layer Execution

8.1 Entity Taxonomy

Entity Owner crate Cardinality Lifetime
ExpertId(u32) moe-core 1..num_experts static (model config)
TokenIdx(u32) moe-core 0..n_tokens per forward pass
CapacityFactor(f32) moe-core 1 per MoE layer static
Capacity(usize) moe-core 1 per expert per pass per forward pass
GateLogits moe-gate 1 per forward pass gate stage → dropped
RoutingDecision moe-gate 1 per token gate stage → dispatch
DispatchPlan moe-dispatch 1 per forward pass dispatch → combine
ExpertWeights moe-expert 1 per expert shard persistent (loaded once)
CombineBuffer moe-dispatch 1 per forward pass dispatch → combine
AuxLossState moe-gate 1 per forward pass gate stage → returned
EpGroup moe-comm 1 per EP group session
MoeError moe-core — transient

8.2 Token Lifecycle State Machine

States a single token passes through in one MoE layer forward pass:

  [INPUT]
     |
     v
  PENDING_GATE ─── gate logits computed ───────────────────────────────┐
     |                                                                   |
     v                                                                   |
  ROUTED (top-k expert ids + weights assigned)                          |
     |                                                                   |
     v                                                                   |
  CAPACITY_CHECK ── per-expert slot available? ─── YES → DISPATCHED    |
                                                 └─ NO  → DROPPED ──────┤
     |                                                                   |
  DISPATCHED                                                            |
     |                                                                   |
     v                                                                   |
  IN_TRANSIT_A2A (moe-comm all-to-all to owning EP rank)               |
     |                                                                   |
     v                                                                   |
  AT_EXPERT_RANK                                                         |
     |                                                                   |
     v                                                                   |
  EXPERT_COMPUTING (grouped GEMM in moe-expert)                         |
     |                                                                   |
     v                                                                   |
  RESULT_IN_TRANSIT (all-to-all back to origin EP rank)                 |
     |                                                                   |
     v                                                                   |
  COMBINING (gate-weighted sum in moe-dispatch::combine)  ◄─────────────┘
     |                                                     (dropped tokens:
     v                                                      residual passthrough)
  OUTPUT

State-transition table:

From To Trigger Invariant checked
PENDING_GATE ROUTED gate forward completes logits finite, no NaN
ROUTED DISPATCHED capacity slot allocated slot_idx < Capacity
ROUTED DROPPED all slots full drop_count tracked in DispatchPlan.dropped
DISPATCHED IN_TRANSIT_A2A a2a fence submitted per_expert_counts symmetric
IN_TRANSIT_A2A AT_EXPERT_RANK a2a completes recv buffer = send counts
AT_EXPERT_RANK EXPERT_COMPUTING grouped GEMM scheduled weight shard local to rank
EXPERT_COMPUTING RESULT_IN_TRANSIT GEMM output buffer ready output shape = [tokens_at_rank, d_model]
RESULT_IN_TRANSIT COMBINING reverse a2a completes token count matches original dispatch
COMBINING OUTPUT combine finishes sum of gate weights over kept experts = 1
DROPPED OUTPUT residual passthrough no GEMM; output = input

8.3 GateLogits Format and RoutingDecision

GateLogits layout: row-major f32, shape [n_tokens, num_experts]
  token_0: [l_0, l_1, ..., l_{E-1}]
  token_1: [l_0, l_1, ..., l_{E-1}]
  ...

RoutingDecision per token (after top-k + softmax-over-topk):
  topk: [(ExpertId(e0), w0), (ExpertId(e1), w1), ..., (ExpertId(e_{k-1}), w_{k-1})]
  invariant: w0 + w1 + ... + w_{k-1} = 1.0 (renormalized over kept experts)
  invariant: e0 < e1 < ... < e_{k-1}  (sorted ascending for determinism)

Gate variants and their mutation of GateLogits:

Variant Mutation before top-k AuxLoss fields used
Dense top-k none importance, load
Noisy top-k add N(0, softplus(W_noise·x)) per expert importance, load
Expert-choice transpose; each expert picks top-c tokens token_counts only
Softmax-all (dense MoE reference) none; k = num_experts none

AuxLossState layout:

  • importance: Vec<f32> — per-expert sum of gate weights over all tokens; len = num_experts.
  • load: Vec<f32> — per-expert fraction of tokens routed; len = num_experts.
  • z_loss: f32 — log(sum(exp(logits)))^2 term (ST-MoE); promotes logit smallness.
  • aux_loss: f32 — dot(importance_norm, load_norm) * alpha (Switch / GShard balance loss).

8.4 Capacity and Drop Semantics

Capacity = ceil(capacity_factor * n_tokens * K / num_experts)

Example: n_tokens=1024, K=2, num_experts=8, capacity_factor=1.25
  Capacity = ceil(1.25 * 1024 * 2 / 8) = ceil(320) = 320 slots per expert

Drop policy (moe-dispatch):

  • Tokens sorted by gate weight descending within each expert queue.
  • First Capacity tokens admitted; remainder marked in DispatchPlan.dropped (BitSet over TokenIdx).
  • Tie-break: if gate_weight equal, lower TokenIdx wins → routing determinism.
  • Dropped tokens: output = input (residual passthrough); combine weight = 0.
  • capacity_factor=inf → no dropping; buffer pre-allocated to n_tokens (unsafe memory use; only for correctness testing).

Edge cases:

  • All tokens route to same expert → (num_experts-1) experts get 0 tokens; valid but inefficient.
  • K=1, capacity_factor=1.0 → exactly balanced expected load; any imbalance causes drops.
  • capacity_factor < 1.0 → always some dropping even in perfectly balanced routing; use only for memory pressure experiments.

8.5 DispatchPlan Layout

DispatchPlan {
  perm:              Vec<u32>,        // length = sum(per_expert_counts); scatter permutation index into input token buffer
  per_expert_counts: Vec<u32>,        // length = num_experts; tokens assigned to each expert after drop
  dropped:           BitSet,          // length = n_tokens; bit set = token dropped
  capacity:          Capacity,        // Capacity value used for this plan
}

Permutation semantics:

  • perm[out_pos] = token_idx where out_pos is position in contiguous per-expert buffer.
  • Per-expert buffer starts at offset[e] = sum(per_expert_counts[0..e]).
  • Invariant: perm contains no duplicates; perm.len() = sum(per_expert_counts).
  • Combine reverse: output[perm[i]] += weight * expert_out[i] (accumulate K contributions per token).

Worked example (n_tokens=4, K=2, num_experts=2, capacity=3):

Token 0 → experts [0, 1], weights [0.7, 0.3]
Token 1 → experts [1, 0], weights [0.6, 0.4]
Token 2 → experts [0, 1], weights [0.5, 0.5]
Token 3 → experts [0, 1], weights [0.9, 0.1]

Expert 0 queue (by weight desc): tok3(0.9), tok0(0.7), tok1(0.4), tok2(0.5)
  → sorted: tok3, tok0, tok2, tok1 (0.9, 0.7, 0.5, 0.4); capacity=3
  → admitted: tok3, tok0, tok2; drop tok1 from expert-0 slot

Expert 1 queue: tok1(0.6), tok0(0.3), tok2(0.5), tok3(0.1)
  → sorted: tok1, tok2, tok0, tok3; capacity=3
  → admitted: tok1, tok2, tok0; drop tok3 from expert-1 slot

per_expert_counts = [3, 3]
perm = [3, 0, 2, 1, 2, 0]   // expert-0 block: toks 3,0,2; expert-1 block: toks 1,2,0
dropped = { tok1 ∩ expert-0, tok3 ∩ expert-1 } — token-level drop only if ALL k slots dropped
  tok1: expert-0 dropped, expert-1 admitted → tok1 still processed (partial drop)
  tok3: expert-0 admitted, expert-1 dropped → tok3 still processed (partial drop)
  → combine: tok1 output = w_{1,1}*exp1_out + residual*(1 - w_{1,1})? NO.
  → SPEC: weights renormalized over admitted experts only; dropped expert contributes 0.
  tok1 admitted only to expert-1 with w=0.6; renormalized weight = 1.0 (only kept expert).
  tok3 admitted only to expert-0 with w=0.9; renormalized weight = 1.0.

8.6 All-to-All Protocol (moe-comm)

EP group topology:

  • EpGroup { rank: u32, world_size: u32, transport: Transport }.
  • Expert e lives on rank e % world_size.
  • Each rank holds ceil(num_experts / world_size) experts' weights.

A2A sequence diagram (4 ranks, 8 experts, 2 experts/rank):

Rank 0 (exp 0,4) | Rank 1 (exp 1,5) | Rank 2 (exp 2,6) | Rank 3 (exp 3,7)
     send_buf           send_buf           send_buf           send_buf
     [for e0,e1,..]     [for e0,e1,..]     [for e0,e1,..]     [for e0,e1,..]
         |                   |                   |                   |
         └────────── all-to-all (MPI_Alltoallv / nccl AlltoAll) ────┘
         |                   |                   |                   |
     recv_buf           recv_buf           recv_buf           recv_buf
     [tokens for         [tokens for         [tokens for         [tokens for
      exp 0,4]            exp 1,5]            exp 2,6]            exp 3,7]
         |                   |                   |                   |
      GEMM exp0           GEMM exp1           GEMM exp2           GEMM exp3
      GEMM exp4           GEMM exp5           GEMM exp6           GEMM exp7
         |                   |                   |                   |
     send_buf2          send_buf2          send_buf2          send_buf2
         |                   |                   |                   |
         └────────── reverse all-to-all ─────────────────────────────┘
         |                   |                   |                   |
     recv_buf2          recv_buf2          recv_buf2          recv_buf2
     (expert outputs back at origin ranks)

A2A message format per rank pair (r_src → r_dst):

  • Header: [token_count: u32, d_model: u32, dtype: u8] (8 bytes).
  • Payload: token_count × d_model floats (f32 or bf16 depending on dtype).
  • Symmetry invariant: tokens Rank A sends to Rank B = tokens Rank B expects from Rank A (enforced by DispatchPlan exchange before a2a).

A2A count-exchange protocol (before data a2a):

  1. Each rank broadcasts per_expert_counts[e] for all remote experts → all_gather counts.
  2. Validate symmetry: rank r_src's send count to r_dst = r_dst's expected recv count from r_src.
  3. Failure: asymmetric counts → MoeError::A2ACountMismatch { src, dst, sent, expected }.

Transport options (moe-comm):

Transport Use case Latency Bandwidth Failure mode
InProc (shared-memory channels) single-node multi-thread EP ~100 ns memory BW thread panic isolation
MPI_Alltoallv multi-node CPU EP ~10–100 µs interconnect rank failure = abort
NCCL AlltoAll multi-node GPU EP ~1–10 µs NVLink/IB NCCL error → reset
Mock (in-process loopback) tests, single-expert 0 — always succeeds

8.7 Comm/Compute Overlap (moe-sched)

Double-buffer pipeline per micro-batch:

Micro-batch 0                    Micro-batch 1
─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
Stream A (comm):  [a2a_fwd_0]─────────────[a2a_bwd_0]
Stream B (compute):            [gemm_0]──────────────────[gemm_0_bwd]
Stream A (comm):                         [a2a_fwd_1]─────────────────
Stream B (compute):                                 [gemm_1]──────────
                   time ─────────────────────────────────────────────►

Overlap invariant: GEMM on micro-batch N starts only after a2a for micro-batch N completes; a2a for micro-batch N+1 can start while GEMM for N runs.

Scheduler state machine:

State Entry condition Actions Exit →
IDLE no pending micro-batches wait on channel GATE_DISPATCH
GATE_DISPATCH input tokens available run gate + dispatch; build DispatchPlan A2A_FWD
A2A_FWD DispatchPlan ready submit a2a send on comm stream; advance buffer slot GEMM_WAIT
GEMM_WAIT a2a event signalled enqueue grouped GEMM on compute stream GEMM_EXEC
GEMM_EXEC GEMM running (optionally submit next micro-batch a2a in parallel) A2A_BWD
A2A_BWD GEMM event signalled submit reverse a2a COMBINE_WAIT
COMBINE_WAIT reverse a2a done enqueue combine kernel OUTPUT
OUTPUT combine done emit output tensor; reset buffer slot IDLE

Double-buffer slot allocation:

  • BufferSlot { id: u8, dispatch_buf: Tensor, result_buf: Tensor }.
  • Two slots per pipeline; slot 0 in GEMM while slot 1 in A2A_FWD.
  • BufferSlotGuard RAII; slot returned to pool on drop.
  • Failure: both slots occupied → scheduler blocks (back-pressure); surfaced as MoeError::SchedulerStall.

8.8 Expert FFN Execution Model

Per-expert computation (single expert, single EP rank):

input_e: [tokens_e, d_model]
  └─ GEMM_1: [tokens_e, d_model] × [d_model, d_ff] → [tokens_e, d_ff]   (w_in)
  └─ activation: SiLU / GeLU / ReLU element-wise → [tokens_e, d_ff]
  └─ GEMM_2: [tokens_e, d_ff] × [d_ff, d_model] → [tokens_e, d_model]   (w_out)
output_e: [tokens_e, d_model]

Grouped/batched GEMM semantics:

  • All experts on rank r are batched into a single grouped GEMM call: [(tokens_e0, tokens_e1, ...), d_model] × expert_weight_stacked.
  • tokens_e = per_expert_counts[e] after a2a; may be 0 (empty expert → skip or zero-pad).
  • Expert weights w_in: [num_experts_per_rank, d_model, d_ff], w_out: [num_experts_per_rank, d_ff, d_model].
  • Sharding: each rank holds only its local experts' weights; no cross-rank weight broadcast.

Zero-token expert handling:

  • If per_expert_counts[e] = 0 for local expert e → skip GEMM for that expert; output slice = empty.
  • Grouped GEMM backend must handle variable-length batches (Megablocks-style block-sparse or padded).
  • Padding to uniform length allowed only for GEMM alignment; padding rows zeroed before combine.

Activation options (configured per model):

Activation Gating variant Memory Notes
SiLU (swish) SwiGLU: d_ff×2 w_gate 2× d_ff intermediate Mixtral, LLaMA-MoE
GeLU standard 1× d_ff Switch, GShard
ReLU standard 1× d_ff Original Shazeer MoE

SwiGLU variant: w_in has shape [d_model, 2*d_ff]; split into gate and value halves; output = SiLU(gate) ⊙ value.

8.9 Combine Stage Semantics

Combine accumulation (after reverse a2a):

output: Tensor[n_tokens, d_model], initialized to zero.
Per permuted position i in [0, perm.len()), with tok = perm[i]:
  weight_i    = routing_decision[tok].weight_for_expert_at_position(i)
  output[tok] accumulates weight_i · expert_results[i]      (sum over admitted experts for tok)
dropped token (all K experts rejected) → output[tok] = 1.0 · input[tok]   (residual passthrough)

Weight renormalization after partial drop:

kept_weight_sum(t) = sum of original_weight[t][e] over admitted experts e for token t
kept_weight_sum(t) == 0.0  → output[t] = input[t]                                  (all k experts dropped t)
kept_weight_sum(t) >  0.0  → effective_weight[t][e] = original_weight[t][e] / kept_weight_sum(t)
                             output[t] = Σ (effective_weight[t][e] · expert_result[t][e]) over admitted e

Numerical invariant: sum(effective_weights) = 1.0 ± 1 ULP for all non-fully-dropped tokens.

Edge cases:

  • k=1, token dropped → full residual passthrough; effective weight vacuously 1.0 (but output = input).
  • k=2, one expert drops → single expert gets weight 1.0 after renorm.
  • capacity_factor=inf → no drops; combine = dense weighted sum; reference implementation path.
  • All tokens dropped (capacity=0 or all experts overloaded) → output = input; warning emitted to telemetry.

8.10 Shared Expert Variant

Shared expert bypass (optional; e.g., DeepSeek-V2 architecture):

  • num_shared_experts: usize experts always receive ALL tokens (no capacity/drop).
  • Shared experts run in parallel to routed experts on the same rank.
  • Shared expert output added to routed combine output: out = shared_out + routed_combine_out.
  • DispatchPlan has shared_expert_ids: Vec<ExpertId> field; non-empty iff shared experts configured.
  • Invariant: shared experts never in dropped BitSet.

8.11 Expert Parallelism Consistency Model

EP rank assignment: expert e → rank e % world_size.

  • Requires num_experts % world_size == 0 OR last rank holds fewer experts (ragged ok; must be handled).
  • Invariant: every EP rank independently validates its local expert IDs match global assignment.

Failure modes:

Failure Detection Recovery
A2A count mismatch pre-a2a count allreduce abort batch; MoeError::A2ACountMismatch
Rank timeout transport deadline (configurable) kill batch; escalate to caller
Expert weight shard missing startup validation MoeError::MissingExpertShard(ExpertId)
NaN in GEMM output optional nan-check kernel post-GEMM MoeError::ExpertNaN(ExpertId)
OOM in dispatch buffer pre-allocation check MoeError::CapacityOom { expert, required, available }

8.12 MoeError Taxonomy

MoeError enum variants (normative list):
  RoutingError(String)              — gate/router internal failure
  CapacityOom { expert: ExpertId, required: usize, available: usize }
  A2ACountMismatch { src: u32, dst: u32, sent: u32, expected: u32 }
  ExpertNaN(ExpertId)              — NaN detected in expert output
  MissingExpertShard(ExpertId)     — weight shard not loaded for expert
  SchedulerStall                   — double-buffer slots exhausted
  TransportError(String)           — underlying comm failure
  ShapeError { expected: Shape, got: Shape }
  DtypeMismatch { expected: DType, got: DType }
  ConfigError(String)              — invalid MoE layer config
  BackendError(String)             — GEMM FFI error

Error propagation: all public API returns Result<T, MoeError>; no panics in hot path; panics allowed only during initialization (weight loading, config parsing).

8.13 Routing Determinism Protocol

Requirements for bit-reproducible routing:

  1. Gate logits computed with same floating-point order (no parallel reduction reordering).
  2. Top-k selection: stable sort by logit descending; ties broken by ExpertId ascending.
  3. Noisy top-k: noise generated from XorShift64(seed=layer_idx * 1337 + token_idx); seed passed in MoeLayerConfig.
  4. Capacity assignment: tokens sorted by gate weight descending per expert; tie-break by TokenIdx ascending.
  5. DispatchPlan.perm built in expert-ascending, then token order within expert.

Non-determinism sources (must be suppressed in deterministic mode):

Source Suppression
GPU atomic reductions use deterministic kernels (cuDNN det flag or software reduction)
Parallel top-k sort use single-threaded stable sort in deterministic mode
NCCL non-det all-reduce use NCCL_DETERMINISTIC=1 env + ring algorithm
FP reassociation fixed reduction order; no auto-vectorizer reordering

8.14 Telemetry Data Model (moe-telemetry)

Per-layer, per-forward-pass metrics:

Metric Type Semantics
expert_utilization[e] f32 tokens_dispatched_to_e / Capacity; 1.0 = full
drop_rate f32 dropped_tokens / (n_tokens * K)
routing_entropy f32 -sum(p_e * log(p_e)) over expert load distribution
aux_loss f32 load-balance auxiliary loss value
z_loss f32 router z-loss value
a2a_latency_us u64 microseconds for all-to-all (fwd + bwd)
gemm_latency_us u64 microseconds for expert GEMM
combine_latency_us u64 microseconds for combine stage
overlap_efficiency f32 (gemm_us) / (gemm_us + exposed_a2a_us); 1.0 = full overlap
tokens_per_expert[e] u32 actual token count dispatched to expert e
shared_expert_tokens u32 tokens sent to shared experts (0 if no shared experts)

Tracing spans (opentelemetry-compatible):

  • moe.gate → moe.dispatch → moe.a2a_fwd → moe.gemm → moe.a2a_bwd → moe.combine.
  • Each span carries: layer_idx, micro_batch_idx, n_tokens, EP rank.
  • Critical path annotation: a2a_fwd duration attributed to comm; gemm duration to compute; overlap = max(comm, compute) vs sequential sum.

9. Tech Stack ADR Table

ADR # Decision Locked Choice Rejected Alternatives Rationale When the Rejected Choice Wins
ADR-01 Implementation language Rust (edition 2021, MSRV 1.78) C++ (safety), Python (perf/control), Go (GC latency), Julia (ecosystem) No GC tail-latency; fearless concurrency; safe FFI to BLAS/CUDA; zero-cost abstractions; portfolio consistency Python wins when team cannot hire Rust engineers and velocity > throughput (research prototype ≤100M tokens/day)
ADR-02 Build system Cargo workspace (10 crates) Bazel (complexity overhead), CMake (C++ heritage), Buck2 (less ecosystem) Native Rust; workspace.dependencies for version pinning; cargo-features for CUDA/MPI feature flags Bazel wins when codebase mixes Rust + C++ + Python in one monorepo and remote caching saves >30 min/CI run
ADR-03 Dependency policy Minimal curated set; no transitive surprises; all deps audited via cargo audit Vendoring all sources (maintenance burden), lock-free open selection cargo.lock committed; cargo deny for license + advisory checks; no yanked crates Full vendoring wins in air-gapped enterprise environments where no outbound network access to crates.io is allowed
ADR-04 GEMM backend FFI cblas-sys (CPU) + cudarc (CUDA) via conditional compilation Manual CUDA bindings (error-prone), BLAS-sys alternatives, tch-rs (too heavy) cudarc provides safe CUDA runtime bindings; cblas-sys for CPU fallback; no full BLAS crate overhead tch-rs wins when the wider PyTorch op set (attention, layer-norm, optimizer) is needed alongside MoE in the same binary
ADR-05 Tensor handle / storage moe-core arena-backed Tensor struct; SmallVec<[usize;4]> for shape ndarray (allocation overhead), tch-rs/candle (heavy deps), raw *mut u8 (unsafe proliferation) Arena controls lifetime; SmallVec avoids heap for ≤4-dim tensors; explicit device/dtype/layout per handle ndarray wins if the project only targets CPU, does not need FFI, and the team values ndarray's slicing/broadcasting ergonomics over raw control
ADR-06 Async runtime tokio (multi-thread) for comm/sched coordination async-std (smaller ecosystem), rayon (compute not IO), smol (insufficient) tokio select! for comm/compute overlap state machine; tokio channels for micro-batch pipeline; well-audited rayon wins when all parallelism is CPU-compute (no I/O, no CUDA events): simpler model, no executor overhead
ADR-07 Intra-process communication tokio::sync::mpsc + crossbeam channels raw mutex/condvar (ergonomics), actor framework (overhead), lock-free queues (complexity) mpsc for pipeline stages; crossbeam for bounded SPSC in hot dispatch loop; both zero-alloc in steady state Actor framework (e.g., Actix) wins when the inter-stage messages carry rich typed variants and supervision/restart semantics are required
ADR-08 Multi-process comm (EP) MPI via rsmpi feature flag; NCCL via cudarc nccl feature; InProc fallback always compiled gRPC (latency), custom TCP (maintenance), UCX bindings (immature Rust) MPI standard for HPC portability; NCCL for GPU bandwidth; InProc for single-node tests; feature flags keep binary size manageable gRPC wins when EP ranks span cloud VMs with heterogeneous network fabrics and the collective volume is modest (<1 GB per forward pass)
ADR-09 Serialization (weights) safetensors (via safetensors crate) GGUF (inference-only tooling), pickle (unsafe), HDF5 (C dep complexity), raw bin safetensors: zero-copy mmap, no code exec, well-adopted in HuggingFace ecosystem; validates dtype/shape GGUF wins in pure-inference mobile/edge deployments where quantization metadata and GGUF-aware loaders are already in the stack
ADR-10 Config format TOML (serde + toml crate) JSON (less human-editable), YAML (ambiguous typing), Protobuf (compile step) TOML: strongly typed via serde; no implicit type coercion; cargo native format; easy to review diffs Protobuf wins when config must be validated across multiple programming languages and versioned API evolution is required
ADR-11 Logging tracing crate (structured, async-aware) log crate (no spans), slog (verbose), env_logger alone (unstructured) tracing: span context propagates across async tasks; instrument macro on hot path; switchable subscriber log crate wins in embedded or WASM targets where tracing's subscriber machinery is too heavy (>50 kB code size budget)
ADR-12 Metrics export metrics crate + metrics-exporter-prometheus OpenTelemetry (heavier), statsd (push-only), custom CSV metrics facade: zero-cost macros; prometheus pull; standard scrape endpoint; no OTLP dep required OpenTelemetry wins when the organization mandates OTLP as the single telemetry pipeline and both traces and metrics must correlate via trace-ID
ADR-13 Distributed tracing opentelemetry + opentelemetry-otlp feature-flagged Jaeger direct (vendor lock), Zipkin (less tooling), custom span log OTLP standard; feature-flagged to avoid mandatory dep; spans cross EP ranks via W3C trace-context propagation Jaeger direct wins when the team runs a self-hosted Jaeger cluster and wants zero OTLP collector maintenance overhead
ADR-14 Testing strategy Unit (cargo test), integration (moe-runtime tests), differential (moe-bench vs dense reference) Property-based only (misses numerical regression), snapshot only (brittle) Differential harness: run MoE with capacity=inf, k=E → must match dense FFN reference within 1e-5 L∞ Property-based only (proptest) wins for pure algebraic components (newtype arithmetic, permutation invertibility) where a reference implementation does not exist
ADR-15 Fuzzing cargo-fuzz (libfuzzer) on DispatchPlan builder and gate inputs AFL (less Rust integration), honggfuzz (manual setup) cargo-fuzz: first-class Rust; fuzz RoutingDecision deserialization and capacity arithmetic for OOB AFL wins when coverage-guided fuzzing must target non-Rust native libraries via shared-memory forkserver (e.g., a vendored C BLAS extension)
ADR-16 Benchmarking criterion (statistical) + moe-bench custom throughput harness hyperfine (CLI only), custom timers (no statistics) criterion: confidence intervals, outlier detection; moe-bench adds token/s and TFLOP/s metrics hyperfine wins for benchmarking end-to-end CLI invocations where process startup cost is the measurement target, not library internals
ADR-17 CI/CD GitHub Actions (matrix: stable, MSRV 1.78, nightly) Jenkins (self-host burden), CircleCI (cost), Buildkite (overkill) Actions: free for OSS; matrix covers MSRV contract; nightly for clippy pedantic + miri subset Jenkins wins when the CI pipeline must run inside a private datacenter with no external network access and custom hardware (GPU nodes)
ADR-18 Sanitizers / Miri ASAN/LSAN in CI (via -Zsanitizer=address) for FFI boundary; Miri for safe Rust core types Valgrind (slow, Linux-only), manual review (insufficient) Miri catches UB in moe-core arena logic; ASAN catches FFI buffer overruns in GEMM dispatch Valgrind wins on non-Linux platforms (macOS CI, x86 FreeBSD) where LLVM sanitizers are unavailable or unstable
ADR-19 Clippy policy #![deny(clippy::all, clippy::pedantic)] workspace-wide; allow list for known false-positives Warn-only (lets issues accumulate), no clippy (regression risk) Pedantic catches cast truncation, missing docs, arithmetic overflow in const eval Warn-only wins during an active major refactor sprint where the allow-list would need to be updated in every PR, slowing merge velocity
ADR-20 Error handling thiserror for all public API errors; anyhow in CLI/bench binaries only Box<dyn Error> (type erasure loss), failure (deprecated), manual Display impls thiserror: zero-cost derive; typed enum variants enable precise match in callers; anyhow for binary entry points Box<dyn Error> wins in glue scripts and one-off tools where error variety is high and the caller will not match variants
ADR-21 Feature flags cuda, mpi, nccl, telemetry-otlp, deterministic Cargo features Compile-time cfg (fragile), runtime-only (binary bloat) Default features = CPU + InProc + prometheus; GPU/MPI opt-in; deterministic flag enables stable sort + det kernels Runtime-only feature detection wins when the binary must run on heterogeneous hardware fleets without recompile (e.g., k8s operator with mixed GPU/CPU nodes)
ADR-22 Reproducibility Cargo.lock committed; cargo vendor snapshot for air-gapped builds; CI hash-pins Actions Floating deps (non-reproducible), Docker-only (no bare-metal CI) Lock file + vendor ensures identical dep graph across machines; hash-pinned Actions prevent supply-chain attack Docker-only wins when binary reproducibility is enforced via container image hash and bare-metal CI is unavailable
ADR-23 Documentation rustdoc for all public API (#![warn(missing_docs)]); FINAL_BLUEPRINT.md as spec Separate wiki (drift), Sphinx (Python toolchain dep), ADRs in Notion (not versioned) rustdoc lives with code; blueprint spec is source of truth; cargo doc --no-deps for offline use Sphinx wins when the project has a large non-Rust audience (ML researchers) who need tutorials, notebooks, and API docs in a single rendered portal
ADR-24 Licensing MIT OR Apache-2.0 (SPDX dual) GPL (incompatible with enterprise FFI deps), MIT-only (some contributors prefer Apache patent clause) Standard Rust ecosystem dual license; safetensors + cudarc + rsmpi all compatible GPL wins when the project is a standalone tool (not a library) and the authors want strong copyleft to prevent proprietary forks
ADR-25 Packaging / distribution Cargo (source); Docker image with CUDA base for GPU benchmarks; no OS packages conda (Python ecosystem coupling), pip (not Rust native), apt/rpm (distro lag) Source distribution via crates.io for library crates; Docker for reproducible GPU environment in CI conda wins when the primary consumers are ML practitioners using conda-forge environments where Python interop and CUDA toolkit version pinning are managed by conda
