(ADR-12) moe-telemetry/src/tracing_layer.rs — tracing-subscriber+tracing-opentelemetry (OTLP gRPC to Jaeger/Tempo); span moe.layer.forward{layer_id} with children moe.gate/.dispatch/.a2a_send/.expert_compute/.a2a_recv/.combine; attributes n_tokens, n_dropped, capacity_factor, k, routing_entropy. (ADR-11, ADR-13) moe-telemetry/src/entropy.rs — routing_entropy(weights: &[[f32;K]], num_experts) -> f32: per-expert marginal = mean gate weight → Shannon entropy, clamped [0, log2(num_experts)]; stored AtomicU32 bit-cast for lock-free reads. Called inside record_routing. moe-telemetry/src/flamegraph.rs — documents CARGO_PROFILE_RELEASE_DEBUG=1 + cargo flamegraph --bin moe-cli; scripts/flamegraph.sh wraps env+invocation+SVG open; flamegraph-dtrace feature for macOS. moe-telemetry/src/snapshot.rs — TelemetrySnapshot (per-step gauge/counter capture, serde_json); snapshot(layer_id) -> TelemetrySnapshot reads atomic state (used by tests + --dump-stats); ring buffer of last 1024 snapshots per layer for Prometheus-free debugging. Integration — moe-gate/src/router.rs calls record_routing+record_aux_loss; moe-dispatch calls record_dispatch post-plan; moe-expert/src/ffn.rs wraps GEMM with Instant::now()→record_expert_compute_ns; moe-sched records a2a timestamps→record_a2a_ns; moe-runtime/src/layer.rs opens/closes moe.layer.forward span. moe-cli — --metrics-addr 0.0.0.0:9100 (Prometheus listener), --otel-endpoint grpc://localhost:4317 (OTLP), --dump-stats path.json (final snapshot), --no-telemetry (compiled-in, runtime-disabled).

12.3 Deliverable

  • moe-telemetry compiles, all probes wired to gate/dispatch/sched/expert; Prometheus /metrics exposes all gauges/counters/histograms; Jaeger shows full per-forward span tree.
  • moe-cli --dump-stats emits TelemetrySnapshot JSON per batch; scripts/flamegraph.sh produces an SVG of a full forward pass; unit tests verify entropy/utilization/drop-rate calculations.

12.4 Acceptance Criteria

Criterion Target
Telemetry overhead vs --no-telemetry ≤ 1% forward-pass latency
moe_expert_utilization accuracy within ±1 token of ground truth
Routing entropy range [0, log2(E)], unit-tested
Drop rate gauge bit-exact match to dropped.count()/n_tokens
Comm/compute ratio within ±5% of manual wall-clock
Prometheus scrape latency < 5ms at 128 experts
Flamegraph SVG generation error-free on Linux (perf) + macOS (dtrace)
OTLP export zero dropped spans at 100 rps synthetic load
--no-telemetry build probes elide to zero instructions (objdump-verified)
Snapshot ring buffer last 1024 steps readable without lock contention

12.5 Conformance Fixtures

Fixture Purpose Expected output
fixtures/telemetry/uniform_routing.json 8 experts balanced routing_entropy≈log2(8)=3.0±0.01
fixtures/telemetry/collapsed_routing.json all tokens→expert 0 entropy=0.0, drop_rate high
fixtures/telemetry/drop_event.json capacity_factor=0.5 drop_rate=expected±1 token
fixtures/telemetry/comm_compute_split.json synthetic a2a=10ms, expert=10ms comm_compute_ratio≈1.0±0.1
fixtures/telemetry/aux_loss_gradient.json known gate weights numeric match to 1e-5
fixtures/telemetry/malformed_snapshot.json truncated snapshot JSON deserialization error, no panic
fixtures/telemetry/replay_1000steps.bin 1000 sequential passes monotonic counters, no resets
fixtures/telemetry/regression_baseline.json locked Phase-11 snapshot no metric regression across refactors

Malformed: negative drop count → clamped to 0 + warning; missing layer_id → deserialization error, not panic.

12.6 Pitfalls

Pitfall Signal Fix
Entropy=NaN moe_routing_entropy=NaN pre-softmax overflow → clamp logits, NaN guard in record_routing
Utilization gauge > 1.0 gauge exceeds 1.0 continuously capacity formula floor-vs-ceil off-by-one, fix per invariant
Prometheus scrape blocks forward pass p99 latency spike during scrape lock-free DashMap/atomic-per-metric, not mutexed gauge map
Span tree missing expert sub-spans Jaeger gap between dispatch/combine explicit _enter=span.enter() guards on non-async path
Flamegraph shows telemetry as hotspot telemetry frames >5% of samples pre-registered metric keys, #[inline(always)] probes (avoid allocating macros)
Ring buffer snapshot stale stale values in --dump-stats AtomicU64::load(SeqCst) for all snapshot reads (torn-read fix)
OTLP drops spans under EP load spans missing in Jaeger, multi-rank increase BatchSpanProcessor queue size + backpressure

12.7 Performance Concerns

Concern Impact Mitigation
Per-expert atomic counter per token N_tokens×num_experts increments batch in stack array, one atomic add per expert
Entropy computation O(N_tokens·K) FP ops post-gate compute on cached gate output, fuse with softmax pass
Prometheus label cardinality 128×100=12800 series limit label dims, aggregate layers at scrape time
OTLP export serialization prost encoding on hot path BatchSpanProcessor async flush, never blocks forward
Ring buffer wraparound cache thrashing (1024-entry circular) 64B-aligned Box<[TelemetrySnapshot;1024]>
Histogram bucket updates Vec allocation per observation pre-allocated fixed-size bucket array
Instant::now() syscall frequency 2 calls/expert-compute + 2/a2a coarse_time (Linux VDSO), negligible overhead

12.8 Edge Cases

  • num_experts=1: entropy=0, utilization=tokens/capacity, valid. n_tokens=0: drop rate defined 0.0, entropy 0.0, record calls skipped.
  • capacity_factor=inf: dropped.is_empty() → drop_rate always 0.0. Layer ID collision across EP ranks: each rank has its own telemetry instance, no cross-rank aggregation in Phase 12.
  • Metrics exporter crash: wrapped in thread::spawn, forward pass continues, error logged. OTLP endpoint unreachable: exponential-backoff retry, otel_export_failures counter, no panic.
  • Snapshot serialization OOM: to_vec returns Err, CLI logs and skips (no abort). Flamegraph without perf/dtrace: fails gracefully with instructions.
  • Routing entropy during noisy top-k: computed on post-noise logits (noise term excluded from marginal).

12.9 Failure Analysis

Failure Detection Blast Radius Recovery
Telemetry crate panic catch_unwind in probe fns single forward-pass step probe returns, runtime continues; fix + redeploy
Prometheus port collision bind() EADDRINUSE CLI exits at startup --metrics-addr picks another port
Snapshot ring buffer corruption torn reads, nonsense values stats output only SeqCst fencing + checksums; flush and restart ring
OTLP exporter OOM unbounded span queue exporter thread OOM-killed bounded queue (1024 max), drop oldest, log
Entropy NaN reaches Prometheus dashboard shows blank panel cosmetic only NaN guard in record_routing; fix gate, restart

Postmortem: on drop-rate spike, check moe_expert_utilization per expert → identify hot expert → inspect capacity_factor → correlate with entropy collapse (often precedes drop-rate surge).

12.10 Operational Notes

  • Metrics/dashboard: Prometheus pull /metrics:9100 (10s interval); Grafana panels for utilization heatmap (expert×layer), entropy timeseries, drop rate, comm/compute ratio.
  • Alerting: drop_rate>0.05, routing_entropy<0.5·log2(E), comm_compute_ratio>3.0.
  • Tracing/logging: 1% sample default (100% via RUST_LOG=trace); ERROR probe panics, WARN NaN/OOM, INFO startup, DEBUG per-step snapshots.
  • Deployment/rollback: library crate, no separate process, no migration on upgrade; --no-telemetry disables at runtime with zero code-path change.
  • Compatibility/security: TelemetrySnapshot versioned (schema_version: u32, serde-default forward-compatible); Prometheus endpoint unauthenticated by default — firewall in production.
  • Flamegraph cadence: run scripts/flamegraph.sh before/after every phase merge, archive SVGs as CI artifacts.

25. Phase 13 — Conformance, differential testing, fuzzing, benchmarks — vs reference dense compute and Tutel/Megablocks semantics; throughput/latency benches

13.1 Objectives

Establish a complete automated quality gate: differential correctness vs a NumPy/PyTorch reference, semantic conformance against documented Tutel/Megablocks behaviors, property-based fuzzing of dispatch/combine/routing invariants, and throughput/latency benchmarks.

Goal Constraint
Numeric correctness vs dense reference max abs error ≤ 1e-4 (f32), ≤ 1e-6 (f64)
Routing conformance with Tutel top-k selection, capacity formula, drop policy identical
Routing conformance with Megablocks expert-choice routing, block-sparse layout equivalence
Fuzz: token conservation, capacity invariant, routing determinism 10k iterations per property, no panic/violation
Throughput bench: tokens/sec at peak EP Criterion; FLOPS utilization reported
Latency bench: p50/p95/p99 forward pass wall-clock; comm vs compute breakdown

Constraints: moe-bench crate owns all benchmark+conformance harness code; Python reference invoked via subprocess or pre-computed fixtures (no Python FFI in Rust); fuzz targets under cargo fuzz/libFuzzer in moe-bench/fuzz/; harness uses only existing public interfaces (no new runtime API).

13.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

Anchors: ADR-14 (differential testing vs dense reference), ADR-15 (cargo-fuzz — token conservation+capacity), ADR-16 (criterion benchmarks), I1 (token conservation, fuzz property), I2 (capacity invariant, fuzz property), P10 (correctness before optimization), I12 (backward gradient validity)

moe-bench/src/differential.rs — DenseReference runs equivalent dense MoE (k=num_experts, capacity=inf) on the same input; dense_forward(input, gate_weights, expert_weights) -> Tensor (sequential experts, gate-weighted sum, no dispatch machinery); compare_outputs(actual, reference, tol) -> DiffResult{max_abs_err, mean_abs_err, rms_err, mismatch_indices}; fixture-driven from fixtures/diff/. (ADR-14) moe-bench/src/tutel_conformance.rs — top-k tie-break (lower ExpertId wins, matches Tutel stable argsort); capacity formula floor(...) (Tutel) vs ceil(...) (this runtime) — documented intentional divergence (ceil preserves capacity invariant on non-divisible inputs); drop policy (lowest-gate-weight overflow) verified against Tutel ordering. Tests: tutel_top2_8experts_32tokens, tutel_capacity_floor_vs_ceil_edge, tutel_drop_tie_break. moe-bench/src/megablocks_conformance.rs — expert-choice routing (ExpertChoiceRouter matches Megablocks documented per-expert top-cap selection); block-sparse GEMM layout (contiguous, zero-padded to block boundary). Tests: megablocks_expert_choice_4experts, megablocks_block_sparse_padding, megablocks_combine_weighted_sum. Fuzz targets (moe-bench/fuzz/fuzz_targets/): token_conservation.rs (arbitrary n_tokens/num_experts∈[1,64]/k/capacity_factor∈[0.1,10.0]+random logits; property: every non-dropped token in exactly k expert buffers, perm.len()==Σper_expert_counts==n_kept·k). capacity_invariant.rs (same input space; per_expert_counts[e]≤capacity for all e, dropped superset of overflow, no OOB in scatter). routing_determinism.rs (two calls to Router::route(logits,k,seed) → identical RoutingDecision; differing seeds logged, not required equal). combine_correctness.rs (arbitrary plan+expert outputs; combine[token]=Σ weight_k·expert_k_output vs scalar reference). (I1, I2, ADR-15) moe-bench/benches/throughput.rs — matrix n_tokens∈{128,512,2048}×num_experts∈{8,32,128}×k∈{1,2}×d_model∈{512,2048}; measures forward wall-clock, tokens/sec, TFLOPS, memory bandwidth; compares EP=1 vs 4-rank simulated EP (InProc); regression gate: no >5% throughput drop vs prior baseline. moe-bench/benches/latency.rs — p50/p95/p99 over 1000 iterations; reports comm/compute/dispatch/combine time via TelemetrySnapshot; scenarios: cold start, warm path (L3-resident weights), large batch (memory-bound). moe-bench/benches/routing_overhead.rs — isolates moe-gate+moe-dispatch cost from GEMM; target < 5% of total forward at num_experts=128. moe-bench/src/harness.rs — BenchHarness{config, weights, input_fixture} shared setup; load_fixture(path) -> InputFixture; run_and_snapshot(layer, input) -> (Tensor, TelemetrySnapshot). CI — cargo test -p moe-bench (differential+conformance, every PR); cargo fuzz run <target> -- -max_total_time=300 (nightly); cargo bench -p moe-bench | tee bench_results.txt + archived Criterion HTML.

13.3 Deliverable

  • moe-bench compiles, all differential/conformance tests pass; fuzz corpus in moe-bench/fuzz/corpus/ clean over 10k iterations; Criterion HTML reports for throughput+latency per matrix entry.
  • bench_results.txt archived in CI with regression gate enforced; Tutel/Megablocks conformance report documents divergences with rationale; dense-reference diff harness reports max abs error per fixture.

13.4 Acceptance Criteria

Criterion Target
Dense reference max abs error (f32) ≤ 1e-4 on all fixtures (capacity=inf, k=num_experts)
Tutel top-k tie-break conformance bit-identical RoutingDecision, 10 fixtures
Megablocks expert-choice conformance token selection matches, 5 fixtures
Token conservation / capacity / determinism fuzz 0 violations, 10k libFuzzer iterations, 0 panics
Combine correctness fuzz max abs error < 1e-5, 10k iterations
Throughput regression gate < 5% degradation vs Criterion baseline
Routing overhead < 5% of total forward pass at num_experts=128
Latency p99 < 2× p50 for warm path
CI fuzz time budget 300s per target, nightly

13.5 Conformance Fixtures

Fixture Purpose Expected output
fixtures/diff/uniform_8e_k2.json dense ref, 8 experts, k=2, capacity=inf max_abs_err ≤ 1e-4
fixtures/diff/random_128e_k4.json 128-expert, k=4, random weights max_abs_err ≤ 1e-4
fixtures/diff/extreme_logits.json logits ∈ [-100,100] no NaN/Inf, max_abs_err ≤ 1e-4
fixtures/tutel/tie_break_8e.json identical logits, two experts RoutingDecision matches Tutel ordering
fixtures/tutel/capacity_edge_32t.json n=33, E=8, cap=1.0 (non-divisible) drop count matches Tutel expected
fixtures/megablocks/expert_choice_4e.json expert-choice, 4 experts token selection identical to Megablocks
fixtures/megablocks/block_padding.json block=32, 17-token batch padding zeros verified
fixtures/fuzz/seed_corpus_conservation.bin libFuzzer seed corpus baseline 0 violations
fixtures/fuzz/seed_corpus_capacity.bin seed corpus, capacity invariant baseline 0 violations
fixtures/regression/phase12_baseline.json locked Phase-12 bench results no regression
fixtures/malformed/negative_n_tokens.json n_tokens:-1 MoeError::InvalidInput, no panic
fixtures/malformed/k_gt_num_experts.json k>num_experts MoeError::InvalidConfig, no panic

13.6 Pitfalls

Pitfall Signal Fix
Dense reference mismatch at large k max_abs_err jumps with k renormalization must divide by sum of top-k weights only, not full sum
Fuzz target OOM libFuzzer OOM kill clamp: assume!(n_tokens<1024 && num_experts<64)
Non-determinism under fuzz "violation" vanishes on replay thread seed explicitly, no implicit global RNG
Criterion warm-up too short high variance in first samples warm_up_time(Duration::from_secs(5))
Tutel capacity divergence treated as bug conformance test fails mark #[known_divergence], document rationale in Appendix
Combine fuzz false positives 1e-6 "violation" from FP associativity ULP/relative comparison, not absolute
Throughput bench non-reproducible Criterion noise > 10% pin CPU core, disable TurboBoost, RUSTFLAGS=-C target-cpu=native
Fuzz corpus not seeded low edge coverage in first 300s pre-seed with fixtures/fuzz/seed_corpus_*

13.7 Performance Concerns

Concern Impact Mitigation
Dense reference O(N·E) vs sparse O(N·K) 16× slower at E=128,K=8 reference only in test mode, not bench hot path
Fixture loading in bench setup disk I/O inflates measured time once_cell::Lazy load, excluded from Criterion timing
Fuzz input deserialization overhead Arbitrary derive cost raw byte slices + manual extraction, no serde in hot loop
Criterion sample count vs wall time 1000×2ms=2s per cell 100 samples / 1s measurement for CI speed
Multi-rank bench uses InProc transport not real NIC documented caveat; real-NIC needs multi-process harness
FLOPS estimation accuracy depends on expert occupancy report both pessimistic (max capacity) and measured
Latency p99 OS-jitter sensitivity no GC but scheduling adds tail black_box; report p999 too

13.8 Edge Cases

  • k=num_experts (dense): reference must be bit-identical, any diff is a bug. capacity_factor=0.0: all tokens dropped, output=input (verified by diff harness).
  • num_experts=1: trivial RoutingDecision, fuzz must cover. n_tokens=1: no batching, included in fuzz corpus.
  • Expert weight all-zeros: combine output = zero tensor, no NaN. Gate logits all-equal: uniform routing, entropy=log2(E), tie-break by ExpertId.
  • d_model=1: degenerate GEMM, must not panic. Fuzz-generated k=0: MoeError::InvalidConfig, no UB (caught via catch_unwind).
  • Replay of identical corpus file twice: identical results (regression fixture detects drift). Bench at EP world_size=1: no a2a, comm time=0.

13.9 Failure Analysis

Failure Detection Blast Radius Recovery
Dense reference diff regression CI fails max_abs_err>1e-4 affects all forward passes block merge, bisect to offending commit
Fuzz crash found libFuzzer reports crash single fuzz target isolate minimized input, fix invariant, add regression fixture
Throughput regression Criterion >5% degradation all throughput-sensitive workloads block merge, profile flamegraph, identify regression
Fuzz OOM libFuzzer kills process fuzz target only add input clamping, re-run
Tutel conformance break conformance test fails routing semantics for deployed models block merge, audit router change, restore/document
Megablocks block-padding mismatch conformance test fails expert-choice routing path flag known divergence or align

Postmortem: on diff regression, check normalization (top-k vs full sum) → dtype (f16 promotion) → activation function (gelu vs relu) → combine weight clipping, in that order.

13.10 Operational Notes

  • CI schedule: differential+conformance every PR; fuzz nightly (300s/target); benchmarks nightly + merge-to-main.
  • Fuzz corpus: committed to moe-bench/fuzz/corpus/, grown by CI merges, pruned quarterly via cargo fuzz cmin.
  • Regression baselines: cargo bench -- --save-baseline new_baseline after intentional perf changes, committed to repo; Criterion HTML archived as CI artifacts, linked in PRs.
  • Divergence registry: DIVERGENCES.md in moe-bench/ (behavior, rationale, test name per entry); dense-reference semantic upgrades re-lock fixtures/diff/ and max_abs_err.
  • Environment/security: bench environment (CPU/memory/NUMA) documented in bench_results.txt header; fuzz targets run under AddressSanitizer nightly.
  • Compatibility: conformance fixtures versioned (fixture_version: u32), harness rejects mismatched versions.

Threat Model

Trust Boundaries and Principals

Principal Trust Level Scope
Operator (process owner) Full Config, weight loading, EP group formation
Config/weight files Partially trusted Validated at load; malformed rejected
Remote EP peers Peer-trusted Same job; authenticated via transport layer
moe-cli user Operator-equivalent CLI args sanitized, no shell injection
GEMM backend (FFI) Trusted Linked at build; supply-chain risk only
Telemetry consumers Read-only observers Metrics endpoint; no write surface

Attack Surface Enumeration

Surface Vector Mitigation Detection Recovery
Config file (JSON/TOML) Malformed TOML → panic; oversized capacity_factor → OOM Schema validation + numeric bounds (0 < cap_factor ≤ 10); hard max per field Startup rejection with MoeError::ConfigInvalid Reject; log offending field; abort
Weight file (mmap) Malformed header → bad shape; truncated → SIGBUS; negative strides → OOB CRC32/SHA-256 header + shape bounds; mmap length check before deref; stride positivity assert Shape mismatch → MoeError::WeightCorrupt Abort weight load; propagate to caller
Weight file (mmap) Crafted strides → memory access outside mmap region Stride validation: stride[i] * (shape[i]-1) ≤ total_numel for each dim Bounds check at Tensor construction Reject Tensor; hard error
GateLogits input Inf/NaN from upstream → top-k produces garbage ExpertIds Pre-gate NaN/Inf check; replace with MoeError::NanInLogits or clamp to [-1e4, 1e4] Telemetry: nan_logit_count counter Clamp + emit warning; optionally zero-route
DispatchPlan (serialized cross-rank) Fabricated counts → capacity overflow on remote rank EP peers are same-job (shared secret / mTLS); counts validated: sum ≤ world_size * Capacity Count mismatch → MoeError::CommProtocolViolation Drop connection; operator alert
All-to-all payload Oversized send buffer → remote OOM Pre-alloc fixed-size double-buffer; reject if per_expert_count > Capacity before send Buffer overflow → panic or OOM Abort layer; log token counts
Noisy top-k RNG Predictable seed → adversarially steer all tokens to one expert (load DoS) Seed from (layer_idx, step_idx, rank); never exposed externally; not user-settable Expert utilization entropy drop → alert Alert; fall back to non-noisy top-k
Replay (stale routing) Reused DispatchPlan from step t at step t+n → wrong outputs DispatchPlan includes (step_id, microbatch_id); moe-runtime validates monotonicity Step-id regression → MoeError::StaleDispatch Reject plan; re-run gate
Resource exhaustion (tokens) n_tokens spike → capacity overflow → mass drops Capacity = ceil(cap_factor * n_tokens * k / num_experts) recomputed each step; drop rate telemetry drop_rate > 0.05 → alert Operator reduces batch; runtime drops gracefully
Resource exhaustion (memory) Expert-weight arena fragmentation → OOM Arena allocates all expert buffers at init; no runtime growth; fixed slab Allocator failure → MoeError::OomExpert Operator reduces num_experts per rank
FFI (GEMM backend) Miscompiled backend writes outside output buffer Output buffer size passed to FFI; backend result validated (non-NaN spot check in debug mode) Spot-check fail → log + error Poison output; propagate error
Supply chain (Cargo deps) Malicious dep version → arbitrary code at build cargo audit in CI; lock file committed; deny.toml banning unmaintained/yanked crates CI audit failure → block merge Pin to audited rev; open security issue
Privilege escalation moe-cli reads /dev/mem or arbitrary paths via config injection Config paths allowlisted to known prefixes; no shell expansion; PathBuf::canonicalize + prefix check Unexpected path prefix → reject at startup Abort; log attempted path
Telemetry endpoint Metric scrape exposes routing internals (traffic patterns) Metrics endpoint on localhost only by default; no routing weights or token content in metrics External scrape → network policy alert Bind to 127.0.0.1; operator opt-in for remote
Persistence corruption Partial weight write → corrupted slab on next load Write-then-rename (atomic); SHA-256 in header verified on load CRC mismatch → MoeError::WeightCorrupt Restore from last good checkpoint

Operator Mistake Vectors

  • Wrong num_experts in config vs. weight file → shape panic at load → clear error, not silent mis-route.
  • capacity_factor=0 → Capacity=0 → all tokens dropped → 100% residual passthrough; telemetry drop_rate=1.0 fires immediately.
  • EP world_size mismatch between ranks → EP consistency invariant violated → MoeError::EpMismatch at group formation.
  • Mixed DType weights (f16 weight, f32 input without cast) → GEMM FFI type mismatch → MoeError::DtypeMismatch.
  • NUMA mismatch (weight on NUMA-0, compute on NUMA-1) → silent latency regression, not a crash; detected by p99 latency alert.

Failure Modes & Recovery

Taxonomy Overview

Class Severity Scope Auto-Recover Manual Required
Routing correctness Critical Layer output No Yes
Capacity / token drop Degraded Output quality Partial (graceful drop) Config tuning
Expert GEMM failure Critical Layer output No Operator
All-to-all comm failure Critical Multi-rank Retry (transient) Operator (persistent)
Weight corruption Critical Startup No Restore checkpoint
OOM (arena / dispatch) Fatal Process No Reduce batch / experts
Scheduler deadlock Fatal Process Watchdog kill Restart
Telemetry failure Degraded Observability only Yes (drop metrics) Alert investigation
Config parse error Fatal Startup No Fix config
FFI panic (GEMM) Fatal Process No (abort) Rebuild backend

Class A — Routing Correctness Failures

  • Symptoms: output logits diverge from reference; combine_correctness test fails; routing entropy collapses.
  • Root causes: NaN in gate logits; incorrect top-k index computation; weight/gate weight normalization bug; combine weights not summing to 1.
  • Detection: differential test (combine_correctness fixture, Phase 6); routing entropy metric < threshold; nan_output_count counter.
  • Auto-recovery: none — wrong output propagates silently without detection.
  • Manual: enable --debug-routing flag → dump RoutingDecision per token; compare against reference gate; check softmax normalization.
  • Forensic artifacts: routing_decision_dump_{step}.bin (enabled via moe-telemetry debug mode).
  • Mitigation: combine_correctness integration test runs on every commit; property tests on gate normalization.

Class B — Capacity / Token Drop Anomalies

  • Symptoms: drop_rate > expected baseline; output quality degrades; dropped tokens take residual (silent pass-through).
  • Root causes: capacity_factor too low for batch size; load imbalance (all tokens route to few experts); noisy top-k seed mismatch.
  • Detection: drop_rate metric per step; expert utilization histogram; aux loss spike.
  • Auto-recovery: graceful drop is by design (deterministic, lowest-weight-first); residual passthrough maintains shape.
  • Manual: increase capacity_factor; tune aux load-balance loss weight; inspect utilization histogram for hot experts.
  • Forensic: dropped_token_ids_dump (debug flag); per-expert token count histogram in telemetry.
  • Mitigation: alert if drop_rate > 0.1 per step; capacity_factor sanity check at config load (warn if < 1.0).

Class C — Expert GEMM Failures

  • Symptoms: MoeError::GemmFailed; NaN/Inf in expert output; layer forward returns error.
  • Root causes: FFI backend segfault or returns error code; mismatched matrix dims; DType mismatch.
  • Detection: FFI return code check; post-GEMM NaN spot check (debug builds); SIGABRT/SIGSEGV caught in panic handler.
  • Auto-recovery: none — layer forward propagates error upward.
  • Manual: check GEMM backend version compatibility; verify weight shape at ExpertWeights construction; run moe-bench with reference backend.
  • Forensic: coredump (ulimit -c unlimited); GEMM input dims logged on MoeError::GemmFailed.
  • Mitigation: backend version pinned in Cargo.lock; dim validation before every FFI call.

Class D — All-to-All Communication Failures

  • Symptoms: layer forward hangs; MoeError::CommTimeout; partial all-to-all leaves ranks in inconsistent state.
  • Root causes: rank failure mid-collective; network partition; buffer size mismatch between ranks.
  • Detection: comm_timeout_ms deadline; heartbeat between EP ranks (moe-comm liveness ping); asymmetric count detection.
  • Auto-recovery: transient timeout → retry up to 3 times with exponential backoff (1s, 2s, 4s); symmetric count re-check after retry.
  • Manual: operator must restart failed rank; rejoin EP group; re-establish transport.
  • Forensic: comm_error_log with rank, step_id, send/recv counts; transport-level error code.
  • Mitigation: heartbeat interval configurable; EP consistency invariant checked before every a2a; operator alert on first timeout.

Class E — Weight Corruption

  • Symptoms: MoeError::WeightCorrupt at load; shape mismatch; CRC failure.
  • Root causes: partial write during checkpoint; filesystem corruption; manual file edit.
  • Detection: SHA-256 + CRC32 header validation at mmap load; shape bound checks.
  • Auto-recovery: none — corrupt weight → abort.
  • Manual: restore from last good checkpoint; re-run weight export if needed.
  • Forensic: log corrupted file path + expected vs. actual CRC; note mtime of file.
  • Mitigation: write-then-rename atomic save; automated restore test in CI (Phase 11).

Class F — OOM (Arena / Dispatch Buffers)

  • Symptoms: MoeError::OomExpert or allocator abort at runtime; process killed by OS.
  • Root causes: expert arena undersized for weights; dispatch buffer overflow from n_tokens spike; double-buffer alloc at peak.
  • Detection: arena pre-check at init; allocator failure → immediate error propagation (no unwinding alloc).
  • Auto-recovery: none for hard OOM; dispatch overflow caught by Capacity invariant (drops tokens, not OOM).
  • Manual: reduce batch size; reduce num_experts per rank; check capacity_factor.
  • Forensic: /proc/PID/status at crash; log arena allocation sizes.
  • Mitigation: arena sizing formula documented in config reference; peak memory estimate in moe-cli --dry-run.

Class G — Scheduler Deadlock

  • Symptoms: process hangs; no progress on telemetry counters; watchdog fires.
  • Root causes: comm/compute overlap misfire (stream waits in cycle); double-buffer swap never triggers.
  • Detection: watchdog timer (default 30s of zero step progress → SIGABRT); step_counter metric stall.
  • Auto-recovery: watchdog sends SIGABRT → coredump for analysis.
  • Manual: operator restarts process; developer inspects stream dependency graph.
  • Forensic: coredump + backtrace; stream dependency ASCII dump (debug flag).
  • Mitigation: stream dependency graph checked for cycles at scheduler init (Phase 9); integration test for overlap correctness.

Class H — Telemetry Failures

  • Symptoms: metrics endpoint returns stale or empty; tracing spans missing.
  • Root causes: metrics thread panic; channel full (backpressure dropped events); exporter connection refused.
  • Detection: telemetry_health counter (self-reported); exporter error log.
  • Auto-recovery: telemetry failures are non-fatal; runtime continues; metrics dropped silently with log warning.
  • Manual: restart metrics exporter; check channel capacity config.
  • Forensic: telemetry_drop_count counter; exporter error log.
  • Mitigation: telemetry errors must not propagate to hot path; channel is bounded (drop-on-full policy documented).

Class I — Config Parse Errors

  • Symptoms: process exits at startup before any forward pass; MoeError::ConfigInvalid on stderr.
  • Root causes: malformed TOML/JSON syntax; missing required field (num_experts, capacity_factor); field out of documented range.
  • Detection: serde deserialization failure with field path; schema validation pass at MoeLayer::new.
  • Auto-recovery: none — fail-fast by design (P6), no partial startup with defaulted-in values.
  • Manual: operator fixes config field named in the error; re-run moe-cli validate-config before deployment.
  • Forensic: rejected config snapshot logged at ERROR with the offending key path.
  • Mitigation: moe-cli validate-config subcommand runs the same schema check in CI before any rollout.

Class J — FFI Panics (GEMM Backend)

  • Symptoms: process aborts (SIGABRT/SIGSEGV) inside the GEMM FFI call; no MoeError returned (panic crosses the FFI boundary as UB).
  • Root causes: ABI mismatch between linked BLAS library and expected symbol signature; backend built against wrong CPU feature set (AVX512 binary on non-AVX512 host).
  • Detection: catch_unwind cannot recover across the FFI boundary; caught only as process exit code ≠ 0 in the parent supervisor.
  • Auto-recovery: none — the process is unrecoverable once corrupted state crosses unsafe FFI; supervisor must restart the process.
  • Manual: verify ldd moe-cli resolves to the expected BLAS .so; rebuild with RUSTFLAGS=-C target-cpu=native matched to deployment hardware.
  • Forensic: core dump (ulimit -c unlimited) plus the last gemm_ffi span's m,n,k dimensions from the pre-crash trace buffer.
  • Mitigation: pin exact BLAS backend version in the deployment image; CI matrix builds/tests on every supported CPU feature level before release.

Operational Readiness

Metrics Strategy

Metric Name Type Labels Alert Threshold
moe_expert_utilization Histogram layer_idx, expert_id p50 < 0.1 or > 0.9 → imbalance
moe_token_drop_rate Gauge layer_idx, step > 0.10 → warn; > 0.25 → page
moe_routing_entropy Gauge layer_idx < 0.3 → collapsed routing
moe_comm_latency_ms Histogram layer_idx, direction (send/recv) p99 > 50ms
moe_compute_latency_ms Histogram layer_idx, expert_id p99 > 100ms
moe_aux_loss Gauge layer_idx > 0.5 → imbalance spike
moe_z_loss Gauge layer_idx > 1.0 → logit explosion
moe_nan_logit_count Counter layer_idx > 0 → alert
moe_nan_output_count Counter layer_idx > 0 → page
moe_comm_timeout_count Counter rank, layer_idx > 3/min → page
moe_dropped_token_ids (debug only) — off by default
moe_step_latency_ms Histogram — p99 SLO
moe_arena_used_bytes Gauge rank > 90% capacity → warn
moe_telemetry_drop_count Counter — > 0 → warn

Tracing Strategy

  • Span hierarchy: MoeLayer::forward → gate → dispatch → a2a_send → expert_batch → a2a_recv → combine.
  • Each span carries: layer_idx, step_id, rank, token_count, expert_count.
  • Comm spans include: send_bytes, recv_bytes, peer_rank.
  • GEMM spans include: m, n, k dims, DType, expert_id.
  • Sampling: 100% in debug/bench; 1% in production (configurable via TELEMETRY_SAMPLE_RATE).
  • Backend: opentelemetry-rust with OTLP exporter; Jaeger-compatible.
  • Tracing failures are non-fatal (drop-on-error, log warning).

Structured Logging

  • Format: JSON lines; fields: timestamp, level, crate, layer_idx, step_id, rank, msg, + typed payload.
  • Levels: ERROR (MoeError propagation), WARN (drop rate, NaN clamp), INFO (startup, EP group formed), DEBUG (per-step routing summary), TRACE (per-token, off in prod).
  • Never log: token content, weight values, gate logit arrays (size + privacy).
  • Log rotation: operator-configured via env MOE_LOG_FILE + MOE_LOG_MAX_MB.

Dashboard Design

Row 1: Expert Utilization Heatmap (layer x expert)  | Token Drop Rate (time series)
Row 2: Routing Entropy (per layer, time series)      | Aux Loss + Z-Loss (time series)
Row 3: Comm Latency p50/p99 (per layer)              | Compute Latency p50/p99 (per expert)
Row 4: Step Latency p50/p99/p999                     | Arena Used Bytes (per rank)
Row 5: NaN Logit / NaN Output counts                 | Comm Timeout counts (per rank)
  • Recommended: Grafana + Prometheus scrape every 15s.
  • Expert utilization heatmap is primary diagnostic for load imbalance.

SLO Definitions

SLO Target Measurement Window Error Budget
Step latency p99 < 200ms (single-rank) rolling 1h 0.1%
Token drop rate < 5% per step rolling 5min 1% of steps
Routing entropy > 0.5 (normalized) rolling 1min < 2% of steps
Comm timeout rate < 1/1000 steps per rank rolling 1h —
NaN output count 0 any window 0

Alerting Philosophy

  • Page (PagerDuty/oncall): NaN output, comm timeout > 3/min, drop_rate > 0.25, step latency p99 > 500ms.
  • Warn (Slack/low-urgency): drop_rate > 0.10, routing entropy < 0.3, aux_loss > 0.5, arena_used > 90%.
  • All alerts include: layer_idx, rank, step_id, link to Jaeger trace, link to Grafana panel.
  • Alert fatigue mitigation: deduplicate identical alerts within 5min window.

Incident Response

  1. Detect: alert fires → check NaN output count first (highest severity).
  2. Triage: routing entropy → drop rate → comm timeout → latency, in that order.
  3. Contain: if NaN output → stop inference immediately; if drop_rate spike → increase capacity_factor without restart (hot config reload, Phase 11).
  4. Diagnose: pull Jaeger trace for failing step → identify which span (gate/dispatch/expert/comm) is anomalous.
  5. Remediate: per runbook (see below).
  6. Post-mortem: structured doc within 48h; update failure mode taxonomy.

Runbooks (per failure class)

  • runbook-nan-output.md: check moe_z_loss; inspect gate logit range; enable --debug-routing; compare expert output against reference.
  • runbook-high-drop-rate.md: inspect utilization heatmap for hot experts; increase capacity_factor; tune aux loss weight; check noisy top-k seed.
  • runbook-comm-timeout.md: check rank liveness; inspect transport logs; restart dead rank; re-form EP group.
  • runbook-oom.md: run moe-cli --dry-run --report-peak-memory; reduce batch or num_experts; check arena sizing formula.
  • runbook-weight-corrupt.md: verify SHA-256 of weight file; restore from checkpoint; re-run weight export if needed.

Backup and Restore

  • Weight checkpoints: write-then-rename; retain last 3 versions; SHA-256 in filename (expert_{id}_{sha256[:8]}.bin).
  • Config snapshots: version-controlled alongside weights.
  • Restore test: moe-cli --verify-checkpoint <path> runs CRC + shape validation without full load; automated in CI (Phase 11).
  • No runtime state requires backup (stateless forward pass); only weights + config.

Deployment Topology

Rank 0: moe-runtime + moe-expert (experts 0, world_size, 2*world_size, ...)
Rank 1: moe-runtime + moe-expert (experts 1, world_size+1, ...)
...
Rank N-1: moe-runtime + moe-expert (experts N-1, ...)
All ranks: moe-comm (EP group), moe-telemetry (local metrics agent)
Operator: moe-cli (single rank or rank 0)

Canary and Rolling Upgrade Strategy

  • Canary: deploy new binary to rank 0 only; validate step latency + drop rate for 100 steps before rolling forward.
  • Rolling: upgrade one rank at a time; EP group tolerates mixed versions only if wire protocol is backward-compatible (see Compatibility Guarantees).
  • Rollback trigger: NaN output on canary rank OR p99 step latency regression > 20% → automatic rollback of canary rank; hold remaining ranks.
  • Rollback mechanism: symlink swap (moe-runtime-active → previous binary); SIGTERM + restart.
  • Mixed-version window: at most 1 rank behind during rolling; enforce via deployment controller check.

Migration Strategy

  • Weight format migration: moe-cli --migrate-weights --from-version V1 --to-version V2; in-place with backup; validates CRC before + after.
  • Config migration: backward-compatible TOML additions only; deprecated keys emit WARN for 2 minor versions then hard error.
  • EP group size change: requires full restart (not a rolling upgrade); operator must stop all ranks, reconfigure, restart.

Compatibility & Versioning

Semantic Versioning Policy

Component Versioning Breaking change triggers major bump
moe-core public types semver Adding required field to ExpertWeights, removing enum variant from MoeError, changing Tensor memory layout
moe-runtime MoeLayer::forward signature semver Changing parameter types, return type, or error variants
Wire protocol (EP all-to-all framing) wire-version header Adding required field, changing count encoding, changing DType encoding
Weight file format format-version u32 in header Changing layout, adding required field, changing CRC algorithm
Config schema (TOML) semver (additive only) Removing required key, changing semantics of existing key
Metrics names/labels semver (additive only) Renaming metric, removing label
CLI flags semver Removing flag, changing semantics of existing flag

Wire Compatibility (EP All-to-All)

  • Every all-to-all frame includes: wire_version: u32 (currently 1), step_id: u64, rank: u32, per_expert_counts: [u32].
  • Backward-compatible additions: new optional fields appended; receivers ignore unknown trailing bytes.
  • Breaking change: increment wire_version; old receivers reject frames with higher version (MoeError::WireVersionMismatch).
  • Rolling-upgrade window: ranks running wire_version V and V+1 may coexist only if the new sender zero-fills new fields (documented in migration note).
  • Mixed-version invariant: wire_version must be identical across all ranks during steady-state; enforced at EP group formation.

Weight File Format Compatibility

  • Header layout: [magic: 4B][format_version: u32][num_experts: u32][d_model: u32][d_ff: u32][dtype: u8][crc32: u32][sha256: 32B].
  • Format version 1: baseline (f32, f16 weight blobs, contiguous row-major).
  • Additive extensions: new optional sections appended after existing blobs; older readers skip unknown sections (length-prefixed).
  • Breaking change: increment format_version; old reader rejects with MoeError::FormatVersionMismatch.
  • Migration tool: moe-cli --migrate-weights handles v1→v2; validates CRC before conversion; writes new file then renames.

API Stability

API Stability Level Policy
MoeLayer::forward Stable (1.0+) No breaking changes without major semver bump
MoeConfig struct fields Stable (1.0+) New optional fields only; deprecation 2 minors before removal
MoeError enum variants Stable (1.0+) New variants non-breaking (exhaustive match warned at compile); removing a variant is breaking
RoutingDecision / DispatchPlan Semistable (0.x) May change in minor versions before 1.0; stabilized at 1.0
moe-telemetry metric names Stable (1.0+) Renaming is breaking; new metrics are additive
moe-bench harness API Internal No stability guarantee; may change in any release
moe-cli flag names Stable (1.0+) No removal without major bump; deprecation warning 1 minor prior

Deprecation Policy

  • Deprecated API: emits #[deprecated(since = "X.Y", note = "use Foo instead")]; compile warning for 2 minor versions.
  • Deprecated config key: WARN log at startup for 2 minor versions; hard error (MoeError::ConfigInvalid) at next major.
  • Deprecated metric name: old name aliased for 2 minor versions; alias removed at next major; documented in CHANGELOG.
  • Deprecated CLI flag: --old-flag accepted with WARN for 1 minor version; removed at next major.

Rolling-Upgrade Guarantees

  • Wire protocol: single version bump per release; N and N+1 wire versions may coexist for at most one rolling step.
  • Weight format: format_version N weights readable by runtime version N and N+1; not guaranteed for N+2.
  • Config schema: new optional fields always have defaults; no required field added in minor version.
  • EP group: all ranks must run same major.minor at steady state; mixed state only during rolling window (one rank behind maximum).
  • Invariant: after rolling upgrade completes, wire_version must be uniform across all ranks (checked by moe-cli --check-ep-versions).

Schema Evolution Rules

  • Add optional field → additive, no version bump; default must be backward-compatible (e.g., capacity_factor new field defaults to 1.25).
  • Remove field → major bump; migration tool must handle old configs with removed field (silently ignore or error, documented per field).
  • Rename field → breaking (major bump); migration tool rewrites config.
  • Change semantics of existing field → major bump regardless of type compatibility.
  • Enum variants in MoeError: adding is non-breaking (clients using _ catch-all are compatible); removing is breaking.

Compatibility Testing

  • Compatibility matrix test in moe-bench: load weight format v1 with current runtime; load format v2 with runtime after migration.
  • Wire compat test: two in-process "ranks" at wire_version N and N+1; verify frame exchange succeeds with zero-fill rule.
  • Config compat test: parse configs from previous 2 minor versions; verify no regression.
  • CI gate: compatibility tests run on every PR that touches moe-core, moe-comm, or weight serialization code.

Testing & Verification Strategy

Unit Tests — Per-Crate Coverage

Crate Key Unit Tests
moe-core Tensor handle arena alloc/free; DType layout stride math; DispatchPlan BitSet ops; MoeError Display/From impls
moe-gate top-k correctness (k=1,2,4,num_experts); softmax normalization sum=1; noisy top-k jitter range; z-loss gradient sign; aux-loss importance/load balance; tie-break by ExpertId ascending
moe-dispatch capacity formula ceil(cfnk/E); drop policy lowest-gate-weight-first; permutation injectivity (no two tokens same slot); combine weights sum-to-1 over kept experts; residual passthrough for dropped tokens
moe-expert FFN shape contract: [tokens,d_in]*[d_in,d_ff]->[tokens,d_ff]; activation fn registry (ReLU/GELU/SiLU); weight shard boundary alignment; grouped-GEMM batch-size-0 guard
moe-comm EpGroup rank/world_size arithmetic; transport enum dispatch; in-proc loopback all-to-all; per-rank count symmetry invariant
moe-sched stream slot allocation; double-buffer index alternation; overlap state machine transitions; micro-batch boundary bookkeeping
moe-telemetry counter monotonicity; histogram bucket boundaries; span nesting depth; export serialization round-trip

Integration Tests — Cross-Crate Pipelines

  • gate→dispatch: feed known logits; assert exact permutation + drop set; verify DispatchPlan.per_expert_counts sum = n_tokens*k − |dropped|.
  • dispatch→expert→combine: single-rank, capacity=inf, k=num_experts; output == dense MLP reference (tolerance 1e-5 f32).
  • comm round-trip: 2-rank in-proc EP; all-to-all tokens out, expert compute, all-to-all back; verify token reconstruction identity.
  • full MoeLayer::forward: moe-runtime orchestration; compare against reference Python (NumPy) output; max abs error < 2e-5.
  • aux-loss + z-loss gradient flow: non-zero grad on gate weights after backward stub; sign matches reference.

Differential / Golden-Value Tests

  • Reference oracle: Python NumPy implementation in tests/reference/moe_ref.py; generates .npy fixture pairs (input, weights, expected_output, expected_aux_loss).
  • Fixtures parameterized: (n_tokens, d_model, num_experts, k, capacity_factor) grid; minimum 48 combinations.
  • moe-bench/src/differential.rs loads fixtures, runs Rust layer, asserts max-abs-error threshold per dtype.
  • Threshold table: f32 ≤ 2e-5; bf16 ≤ 5e-3; f16 ≤ 1e-3.
  • Regenerate fixtures with make gen-fixtures; fixtures committed to tests/fixtures/ (LFS for >1 MB).

Property-Based Tests (proptest / quickcheck)

  • moe-gate: forall logit matrix → topk indices distinct, weights ∈ [0,1], weights sum ≤ 1+ε.
  • moe-dispatch: forall DispatchPlan → no slot index ≥ Capacity; every non-dropped token appears exactly k times in perm.
  • moe-dispatch combine: forall combine weights → output norm ≤ input_norm * max_gate_weight (no amplification).
  • Token conservation property: |kept| + |dropped| == n_tokens * k.
  • Routing determinism: same RNG seed + inputs → identical RoutingDecision bit-for-bit across 100 runs.
  • Capacity invariant: forall capacity_factor ∈ (0.5, 2.0), n_tokens ∈ [1, 4096] → Capacity formula monotone in cf.

Fuzzing (cargo-fuzz / libfuzzer)

Fuzz Target Input Invariant Checked
fuzz_dispatch_plan arbitrary bytes → DispatchPlan deserialization no panic, no OOB
fuzz_gate_logits f32 array with NaN/Inf no panic; MoeError::InvalidLogits returned
fuzz_config_parse arbitrary TOML bytes no panic; parse either Ok or Err
fuzz_comm_packet arbitrary bytes → all-to-all recv path no panic; corrupt packet → MoeError
  • Fuzz corpus in fuzz/corpus/; CI runs 60-second fuzz on each PR.
  • Address sanitizer + UB sanitizer enabled in fuzz profile.

Chaos / Fault-Injection Tests

  • Rank death simulation: kill EP rank mid-a2a; assert MoeError::CommFailure propagates cleanly, no deadlock within 5 s timeout.
  • Capacity overflow injection: force all tokens to same expert; assert drop policy fires; output shape unchanged; drop_rate metric increments.
  • OOM injection: override allocator; assert graceful MoeError::OutOfMemory, no partial write to output buffer.
  • Slow expert: inject 100 ms sleep in expert 0 FFN; assert scheduler timeout path; verify other experts not blocked.

Deterministic Replay

  • moe-telemetry records ReplayLog { seed, config, input_hash, routing_decision_hash, output_hash } in debug builds.
  • moe-cli replay <log> re-runs exact session; asserts hash match.
  • Replay harness used to reproduce flaky routing bugs reported from production traces.

Soak / Long-Duration Tests

  • 1-hour soak: continuous MoeLayer::forward at batch_size=128, num_experts=64, k=2; monitor RSS growth < 1 MB/hr (leak check).
  • Metric drift test: expert utilization std-dev must not widen over 1-hour run (load balance stability).
  • Soak runs nightly in CI; alert on >5 % memory growth.

Performance Regression

  • moe-bench emits criterion JSON; CI compares against main baseline; fail if >5 % latency regression on any tracked scenario.
  • Tracked scenarios: (n=2048,E=8,k=2), (n=4096,E=64,k=2), (n=512,E=128,k=4).
  • Regression results posted as PR comment by CI bot.

Conformance Validation

  • Gate output validated against Switch Transformer paper Eq. 1 (top-1 softmax) and GShard top-2 formulas.
  • Aux-loss value validated against Fedus 2021 Eq. 4 at known utilization vectors.
  • Z-loss value validated against ST-MoE paper Eq. 2 with known logit magnitudes.

Build & Release Engineering

Reproducible Builds

  • Cargo.lock committed; --locked flag mandatory in all CI and release builds.
  • SOURCE_DATE_EPOCH injected at build time; binary timestamps deterministic.
  • No build.rs env captures beyond explicit allowlist (RUSTC_VERSION, GIT_HASH, BUILD_DATE).
  • Cross-compile targets: x86_64-unknown-linux-gnu, aarch64-unknown-linux-gnu; built in identical Docker image (rust:1.78-slim).
  • Docker image pinned by digest (not tag); image hash in Makefile and CI YAML.
  • cargo vendor snapshot in vendor/; offline builds possible from snapshot.

SBOM Generation

  • cargo-sbom generates CycloneDX JSON per build; artifact: moe-runtime-<ver>.cdx.json.
  • SBOM includes: crate name, version, license, source URL, checksum.
  • SBOM diff gated in CI: new crate additions require explicit approval step.
  • cargo-deny check: license allowlist (MIT/Apache-2.0/BSD); deny GPL/LGPL unless explicitly exempted.
  • cargo-audit runs on every PR and nightly; RUSTSEC advisories block merge.

Artifact Signing

  • Release binaries signed with cosign (sigstore); signature stored in OCI registry alongside artifact.
  • Signing key: hardware-backed (YubiHSM); private key never touches CI runner disk.
  • cosign verify command documented in SECURITY.md; consumers verify before deployment.
  • Checksum file SHA256SUMS attached to every GitHub release; signed by same key.

Release Channels

Channel Trigger Stability Version Scheme
nightly main-branch push unstable 0.0.0-nightly.YYYYMMDD
beta release-branch tag vX.Y.0-beta.N pre-release semver pre-release
stable tag vX.Y.Z production semver
  • CHANGELOG.md maintained; entries per unreleased/stable section.
  • Breaking API changes require minor-version bump; no silent breakage in patch.

CI/CD Topology

  • PR push
    • ├── lint (clippy --deny warnings, rustfmt --check)
    • ├── test (cargo test --locked, all crates, ASAN/LSAN)
    • ├── bench-regression (criterion compare vs main)
    • ├── fuzz-short (60 s per target)
    • ├── sbom-diff + cargo-deny + cargo-audit
    • └── differential (fixture comparison)
  • main push
    • ├── all PR steps
    • ├── soak-short (10 min)
    • └── nightly artifact publish
  • release tag
    • ├── cross-compile (linux/x86_64, linux/aarch64)
    • ├── reproducibility check (build twice, diff binaries)
    • ├── sbom + sign
    • ├── github release + checksum upload
    • └── cosign sign + push to OCI
  • CI runner: GitHub Actions; runner image pinned by digest.
  • Secrets: COSIGN_KEY stored in GH Actions secrets; never printed to logs.
  • Cache: cargo-cache action; keyed on Cargo.lock hash.

Version Stamping

  • build.rs in moe-cli injects: GIT_HASH (short), BUILD_DATE (ISO-8601), RUSTC_VERSION.
  • moe-cli version prints all three; moe-runtime exposes Runtime::build_info() -> BuildInfo.
  • Version embedded in telemetry span build.hash attribute for correlation.

Dependency Auditing

  • cargo-deny policy in deny.toml; blocks: duplicate versions of core crates, yanked versions.
  • Dependency update PRs via Dependabot (weekly); auto-merge for patch if CI green.
  • cargo tree --duplicates must be empty for serde, tokio, crossbeam at release.

Binary Provenance

  • SLSA Level 2: build provenance attestation generated by slsa-github-generator; stored alongside artifact.
  • Provenance JSON: builder ID, workflow ref, source digest, build trigger.
  • Consumers can verify with slsa-verifier verify-artifact.

Performance Engineering

Benchmarking Strategy

  • moe-bench crate: criterion micro-benchmarks + wall-clock integration benchmarks.
  • Benchmark taxonomy:
Level Fixture Measured
Micro single gate pass, n=512, E=8 gate latency μs
Micro dispatch plan build, n=2048, E=64 dispatch μs
Micro GEMM FFI call, batch=128, d=4096 GEMM μs, FLOP/s
Micro all-to-all loopback, world=4 a2a latency μs
Integration full MoeLayer::forward, multiple configs end-to-end ms
Soak 1000 forward passes, n=4096 p50/p95/p99/p999 ms
  • Warm-up: 10 iterations discarded; measurement: 100 iterations minimum.
  • criterion configured: measurement_time = 10s, sample_size = 100.
  • Results stored as JSON in benches/baseline/; diff script in scripts/bench_compare.py.

Throughput Targets

Config Target p50 Target p99
n=2048, E=8, k=2, d=4096 ≤ 2 ms ≤ 4 ms
n=4096, E=64, k=2, d=4096 ≤ 8 ms ≤ 15 ms
n=512, E=128, k=4, d=4096 ≤ 5 ms ≤ 12 ms
  • Targets measured on: Intel Xeon Gold 6338, 2 sockets, 256 GB DDR4; no GPU.
  • GPU path targets TBD post CUDA backend integration.

Quantified Performance Targets (gated in CI)

M# Metric Target Phase Gate
M1 Forward latency p50 (n=2048, E=8, k=2, d=4096) ≤ 2 ms Phase 5
M2 Forward latency p99 (n=2048, E=8, k=2, d=4096) ≤ 4 ms Phase 5
M3 Forward latency p50 (n=4096, E=64, k=2, d=4096) ≤ 8 ms Phase 6
M4 Forward latency p99 (n=4096, E=64, k=2, d=4096) ≤ 15 ms Phase 6
M5 GEMM throughput (512 tok, d_model=1024, d_ff=4096, CPU) ≥ 50 GFLOPS Phase 5
M6 InProc a2a throughput (4 ranks, 1 MB total) > 2 GB/s Phase 7
M7 InProc a2a latency (4 ranks, 1 KB total) < 20 µs Phase 7
M8 Comm/compute overlap fraction (M=2, balanced workload) ≥ 0.4 Phase 8
M9 L3 cache hit rate, dispatch scatter-gather (capacity ≤ 4096) > 90% Phase 8
M10 Heap allocations inside forward hot path 0 Phase 5
M11 Routing overhead as fraction of total forward pass (E=128) < 5% Phase 9
M12 Telemetry overhead vs --no-telemetry baseline ≤ 1% Phase 12

Regression gate: cargo bench -p moe-bench compares every M# against its stored Criterion baseline; a build fails CI if any M# regresses beyond its stated target (Phase 13 harness, §13.4).

Profiling Methodology

  • CPU profiling: cargo flamegraph (--bin moe-cli --features profiling); perf record -g on Linux.
  • Flamegraph workflow: flamegraph -o out.svg -- moe-cli bench --config configs/profile.toml; annotate hot paths.
  • Expected hot paths: GEMM FFI dispatch, permutation scatter-gather, softmax normalization, all-to-all memcpy.
  • Tracing: moe-telemetry emits tracing spans; export via OTLP to Jaeger; visualize span durations in Jaeger UI.
  • Span hierarchy: MoeLayer::forward → gate, dispatch, a2a_send, expert_batch, a2a_recv, combine.
  • RUST_LOG=moe_telemetry=trace enables per-token routing trace (dev only; 100x overhead).

Allocator Analysis

  • Default: system allocator (glibc malloc); switch to tikv-jemallocator for arena profiling.
  • Enable jemalloc prof: _RJEM_MALLOC_CONF=prof:true,prof_prefix:/tmp/heap; dump every N bytes.
  • jeprof generates heap flamegraph; compare before/after buffer pooling changes.
  • Target: zero heap allocs in hot path after Phase-5 buffer pool; verify with dhat or heaptrack.
  • Arena reuse: moe-core tensor arena; measure arena hit rate via telemetry::arena_hits_total counter.

Lock / Contention Analysis

  • moe-sched uses lock-free channels (crossbeam-channel); contention measured via perf lock on Linux.
  • Any Mutex in hot path is a bug; lint via clippy::mutex_in_unsafe_block + code review.
  • std::sync::atomic ordering: SeqCst only for scheduler state machine; Relaxed for counters.
  • Lock-free queue depth histogram in moe-telemetry: scheduler.queue_depth gauge.

Cache Analysis

  • perf stat -e cache-misses,cache-references on dispatch scatter-gather; target: >90 % L3 hit rate for capacity ≤ 4096.
  • Permutation buffer layout: tokens contiguous per expert (struct-of-arrays for GEMM); avoid strided access.
  • Expert weight locality: weights pinned to NUMA node matching compute thread; numactl wrapper in moe-cli.
  • False-sharing: CombineBuffer padded to 64-byte cache lines; verified with perf c2c.

Syscall Analysis

  • strace -c -p <pid> on soak run; target: zero syscalls in per-token hot path.
  • Allowed syscalls: mmap for initial arena setup; futex for thread sync; write for telemetry flush.
  • Forbidden in hot path: malloc/free (arena path), open/stat (weights preloaded), clock_gettime (use TSC via quanta).

Tail-Latency Analysis

  • HDR histogram in moe-telemetry: forward_latency_us with 3 significant figures.
  • Report p50/p95/p99/p999/max per batch; export as Prometheus histogram.
  • Outlier investigation: spans with forward_latency_us > p999 tagged slow=true; dump RoutingDecision for those batches.
  • Jitter sources: OS scheduling (pin threads via core_affinity), NUMA migration (lock pages), GEMM backend variability.

Memory Profiling

  • valgrind --tool=massif for heap profile over soak run; ms_print for peak heap identification.
  • Track: per-expert buffer peak, CombineBuffer peak, telemetry ring buffer, permutation Vec growth.
  • Memory budget per layer: 2 * n_tokens * d_model * sizeof(f32) input+output + n_experts * capacity * d_model * sizeof(f32) dispatch buffers.
  • Budget formula enforced as assert in moe-dispatch::allocate_buffers.

Scalability Analysis

Bottleneck Map

Scaling dimension      Primary bottleneck           Secondary
──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
n_tokens ↑            dispatch permutation O(n·k)   GEMM batch dim (good)
num_experts ↑         all-to-all volume O(E)         gate softmax O(E)
k (top-k) ↑           dispatch buffer O(n·k)         combine weighted sum
EP world_size ↑       all-to-all latency O(W)        rank-count imbalance
d_model ↑             GEMM compute (linear)           weight memory O(E·d²)
d_ff ↑                GEMM compute (linear)           per-expert memory
capacity_factor ↑     buffer memory O(cf)             drop rate ↓ (good)
layers ↑              pipeline memory O(L)            sched queue depth

Horizontal Scaling — Expert Parallelism

  • Scale-out: add EP ranks; expert count fixed, experts redistributed (expert e → rank e % W).
  • All-to-all volume per rank: n_tokens * k * d_model * sizeof(f32) / W send + equal recv.
  • Scaling efficiency: linear GEMM throughput, sublinear comm (volume/rank constant), latency bounded by a2a latency floor.
  • Ceiling: world_size ≤ num_experts (each rank must own ≥ 1 expert); beyond this, ranks idle.
  • Practical ceiling: NVLink/InfiniBand bandwidth ÷ a2a message size; typically 512–1024 ranks for E=1024.

Horizontal Scaling — Data Parallelism (future phase)

  • DP replicates entire MoE layer; all-reduce over gate params only (not expert weights, which are EP-sharded).
  • Gradient all-reduce volume: num_experts * d_model * sizeof(f32) per layer — small relative to activations.
  • Expert weight gradient: reduce within EP group only; no cross-DP-rank expert weight sync needed.

Vertical Scaling

  • Larger d_model/d_ff: GEMM scales O(d²); memory scales O(E * d_model * d_ff); single biggest lever is GEMM backend parallelism (OpenBLAS threads, CUDA SM occupancy).
  • Larger n_tokens: dispatch+combine O(n*k); GEMM naturally batched; diminishing returns in comm overlap above n=8192 (comm hides behind GEMM).
  • CPU core count: dispatch/combine embarrassingly parallel over tokens; GEMM backend uses all cores; scheduler streams scale with core count.

Sharding and Partitioning

  • Expert sharding: round-robin expert_id % world_size; alternative consistent-hashing rejected (load imbalance under non-uniform expert popularity).
  • Weight sharding within expert (tensor parallelism): future phase; requires additional all-reduce inside FFN.
  • Activation sharding: tokens split across DP ranks; no cross-shard dependency before all-to-all.
  • DispatchPlan partitioned per rank: each rank builds only its send/recv plan slice; O(n_tokens/W) local work.

Replication Pressure

  • Expert weights NOT replicated across EP ranks (purpose of EP); only gate matrix W_g replicated across all ranks.
  • Gate replication cost: num_experts * d_model * sizeof(f32) per rank; for E=128, d=4096: 2 MB — negligible.
  • CombineBuffer NOT replicated; local per rank; scales with local token count.

Coordination Overhead

  • All-to-all barrier: hard synchronization point; W ranks must all reach a2a simultaneously.
  • Straggler penalty: slowest rank determines a2a completion; load imbalance in expert compute creates straggler.
  • Mitigation: capacity factor limits expert buffer size; moe-sched timeout-based straggler detection (configurable comm_timeout_ms).
  • AuxLoss all-reduce: one scalar all-reduce per forward pass; O(W) latency, negligible bandwidth.
  • Telemetry aggregation: async, non-blocking; never on critical path.

Network Fanout

  • All-to-all pattern: each rank sends to all W ranks, receives from all W ranks; W² message pairs.
  • Message size per pair: (n_tokens / W) * k * d_model * sizeof(f32) (assuming uniform routing).
  • Total bandwidth per rank: (W-1) * (n_tokens/W) * k * d_model * 4 bytes bidirectional.
  • Example (W=8, n=4096, k=2, d=4096): ~128 MB/rank per forward pass; 10 GbE = 12.8 ms; 100 GbE = 1.28 ms; NVLink 300 GB/s = 0.43 ms.
  • Bandwidth ceiling dominates at W > 32 without high-speed fabric.

Scheduler Saturation

  • moe-sched double-buffer: 2 micro-batches in flight; saturation when GEMM time < a2a latency (comm-bound).
  • Queue depth metric: scheduler.queue_depth histogram; p99 > 4 → add micro-batch slots.
  • Thread pool sizing: num_experts / world_size compute threads per rank; excess threads waste context switches.
  • Stream contention: if GEMM backend uses all CPU cores, dispatch/combine threads starve; isolate via core_affinity pinning.

Storage Growth

  • Weight storage: num_experts * d_model * d_ff * 2 * sizeof(f32) per layer (w_in, w_out); for E=64, d=4096, d_ff=16384: 128 GB total, 2 GB/rank at W=64.
  • Checkpoint size grows linearly with E; distributed checkpoint (one file per rank) avoids single-node bottleneck.
  • Telemetry retention: histogram snapshots 1 KB/forward pass; ring buffer capped at 64 MB (configurable); flush to disk async.
  • Replay log: 64 bytes per forward pass header + hash; 10 M passes = 640 MB; rotate at configurable limit.

Metadata Scaling

  • RoutingDecision: n_tokens * k * 8 bytes (ExpertId u32 + f32 weight) per forward pass; for n=4096, k=2: 64 KB — fits in L2.
  • DispatchPlan: n_tokens * k * 4 bytes permutation + num_experts * 4 bytes counts; for n=4096, k=2, E=128: 33 KB.
  • No persistent metadata store; all routing state ephemeral per forward pass; no distributed coordinator needed.
  • AuxLossState accumulates over micro-batches; scalar per expert; num_experts * 4 bytes = 512 B for E=128 — negligible.
  • Config metadata: single TOML file; no distributed config service required; loaded once at startup.

Mental Models & First Principles

MM1 — Sparse routing is selective broadcasting, not filtering. The compressed insight: each token is sent to exactly k out of E experts, not routed away from E-k experts. The gate weight is a mixture coefficient, not a confidence score. What it predicts: capacity overflow happens because k assignments per token × n_tokens tokens creates k×n_tokens expert "slots" competing for E×C total capacity; at uniform routing each expert sees n_tokens×k/E arrivals, and the capacity formula is just that number rounded up. Bug class without it: developers who think of routing as "filtering" set capacity_factor=1.0 thinking it should be sufficient, then see 15%+ drop rates because they did not account for the ceil and non-uniform load variance. Resolving §: §4.2 capacity formula, §6.3 (P3), §7 I2.

MM2 — All-to-all is a symmetric data transpose, not a send/receive. Each rank holds a row-slice of the token×expert assignment matrix; after all-to-all each rank holds a column-slice (all tokens for its experts). Token buffer sizes before and after a2a are determined by the count arrays, not the token count. What it predicts: every "why is rank r receiving more bytes than it sent?" is answered by load imbalance — send-counts to rank r from all peers sum to its expert load. Bug class without it: asymmetric count bugs where send_counts[r→s] ≠ recv_counts[s←r] cause silent buffer overruns or hangs; I10 invariant catches them. Resolving §: §7 I10, Phase 5 (a2a.rs), Phase 7 (Transport trait).

MM3 — Capacity factor is a queuing system parameter, not a throughput knob. Setting capacity_factor=1.0 is analogous to a zero-wait-time queue: any arrival imbalance causes drops. Capacity_factor=1.25 provides ~25% headroom above mean load. What it predicts: at k=2, uniform routing, E=64, n=4096, the mean per-expert load is 128 tokens; std dev ≈ sqrt(n×k/E) ≈ 11; 3σ above mean ≈ 161; capacity_factor ≥ 1.26 ensures zero drops at 3σ. Bug class without it: capacity_factor tuned by intuition rather than load distribution statistics; sporadic drops under bursty inputs. Resolving §: §4.2, §§ Back-of-Envelope Capacity Model.

MM4 — Comm/compute overlap requires explicit event ordering, not async fire-and-forget. The CUDA event model requires: (1) compute stream records event A after GEMM done, (2) comm stream waits on event A before launching a2a, (3) comm stream records event B after a2a done, (4) compute stream waits on event B before next GEMM. Dropping any event causes data races or hangs. What it predicts: overlap efficiency drops to zero if the comm stream begins sending while GEMM is still writing the source buffer. Bug class without it: random numerical corruption at high batch sizes where timing luck normally hides the race. Resolving §: §6.8 (P8), §7 I6, Phase 8 (doublebuf.rs).

MM5 — Expert-choice routing inverts the capacity constraint direction. In token-choice routing, each token picks top-k experts (E constrains tokens). In expert-choice, each expert picks top-c tokens (tokens constrain experts). Expert-choice eliminates token drops entirely but requires the full n_tokens × E score matrix to be materialized before any dispatch can start — a synchronization barrier absent in token-choice. What it predicts: expert-choice is strictly better for training stability but adds a full matrix barrier latency that token-choice avoids. Bug class without it: implementing expert-choice with the token-choice DispatchPlan pipeline causes wrong assignment counts because the assignment is column-major not row-major. Resolving §: §3.4, Phase 9 (expert_choice.rs), §§ Alternative Architectures.

MM6 — Token conservation is a two-part invariant: routing path AND residual path. It is not enough to route every token; every dropped token must explicitly take the residual passthrough path (output[t] = input[t]). A single missing residual assignment causes a zero-row in the output tensor — numerically valid but semantically wrong, and the model loss degrades slowly without a clear signal. What it predicts: any test that checks only "no panic" or "output shape matches input" will miss a missing residual. Bug class without it: tokens silently zeroed under capacity overflow; detected only by differential test against dense reference. Resolving §: §7 I1, §6.1 (P1), Phase 3 (combine.rs residual passthrough).

Comparative Architecture Table

This runtime vs four real deployed MoE systems across six load-bearing dimensions.

Dimension This Runtime GShard (Lepikhin 2020) Switch Transformer (Fedus 2021) Mixtral 8×7B (Jiang 2024) DeepSpeed-MoE (Rajbhandari 2022)
Routing Top-k (k≥1), expert-choice, noisy-topk, hash — pluggable via RouterStrategy Top-2 token-choice; capacity factor α; noisy top-2 Top-1 token-choice; simplified capacity = 2×n/E Top-2 token-choice; no aux loss at inference Top-1 or top-2; residual expert (shared)
Capacity enforcement ceil(capacity_factor × n_tokens × k / E); deterministic drop by lowest gate weight; residual passthrough α × (n_tokens / E); drop by lowest; tokens without any expert: 0-weight residual 2 × (n_tokens / E); no noise; aggressive drop tolerance capacity_factor not published; token-drop acknowledged in paper Dynamic capacity; PR-MoE adds residual experts to absorb overflow
Expert parallelism Pure EP: expert_id % world_size; pluggable Transport (InProc/MPI/NCCL) XLA mesh-based EP; fused with data parallelism in TPU layout EP via model parallelism axis; not separated from TP Hugging Face Transformers EP via device_map; no native runtime Hierarchical EP: node-level + rack-level; reduces cross-node a2a
Comm/compute overlap Explicit double-buffer in moe-sched; comm stream + compute stream with CUDA events XLA handles overlap via HLO pipeline; not user-visible Not implemented; a2a and FFN serialized Framework-dependent; no dedicated overlap layer Tutel-inspired overlap via adaptive pipeline depth
Load balance mechanism Aux importance loss (P1) + smooth load loss (P2) + z-loss (P5); coefficients in MoeConfig Two-term aux loss: importance × load; α coefficient Single-coefficient aux loss; simpler formula Aux loss during training; disabled at inference Aux loss + capacity_factor tuning; PR-MoE residual absorbs stragglers
Weight storage safetensors zero-copy mmap; per-expert sharded; int8/fp8 quantization in Phase 10 TPU HBM weights managed by XLA; no mmap Checkpoint-based; no mmap; standard T5X format GGUF or safetensors depending on serving stack DeepSpeed checkpoint sharded by rank; ZeRO-Inf for CPU offload

GShard (TPU-native, 600B parameters, 2020): pioneered the capacity-factor + deterministic-drop-by-weight contract that this runtime implements verbatim. Key architectural lesson: symmetric count protocol for all-to-all is load-bearing — GShard's TPU compiler enforces it via shape analysis; this runtime enforces it via I10 invariant test. Pitfall not copied: global singleton communicator that cannot be safely torn down mid-process.

Switch Transformer (1.6T, JMLR 2022): proved k=1 routing is viable with careful capacity_factor tuning (typically 1.0–1.25 at k=1). Key lesson: the k=1 boundary case is a stress test for the gate, not a degenerate case — this runtime explicitly tests it in Phase 2. Pitfall not copied: training instability at capacity_factor below 1.0 is managed by loss clipping in Switch; this runtime returns MoeError::CapacityOverflow instead.

Mixtral 8×7B (arXiv 2401, 2024): production k=2, 8-expert MoE in a sliding-window attention model. Key lesson: realistic capacity_factor at inference is 1.0–1.5; the §4 system constraints in this blueprint use Mixtral-scale numbers as the reference point. Pitfall not copied: Mixtral's capacity_factor is not published; this runtime requires explicit configuration rather than silent defaults.

DeepSpeed-MoE (ICML 2022): hierarchical EP and PR-MoE (residual expert) for very large EP worlds. Key lesson: the shared/residual expert pattern (Phase 9) absorbs routing variance without increasing capacity_factor; reduces drop rate at the cost of one extra dense FFN pass per token. Pitfall not copied: Python-level dispatch loop adds 50–100 µs per layer; this runtime enforces Rust-only hot path.

Back-of-Envelope Capacity Model

Starting from §4 System Constraints (E=64, k=2, d_model=4096, d_ff=16384, BF16, n_tokens=2048, EP world_size=8, capacity_factor=1.25).

1. Per-expert token budget:

capacity = ceil(1.25 × 2048 × 2 / 64) = ceil(80.0) = 80 tokens/expert/rank

2. Local expert count per rank:

local_experts = 64 / 8 = 8 experts per rank

3. VRAM for expert weights per rank (BF16):

w_in: [d_ff, d_model] = [16384, 4096] × 2 bytes = 128 MB per expert
w_out: [d_model, d_ff] = [4096, 16384] × 2 bytes = 128 MB per expert
per_expert_weights = 256 MB
local_weights = 8 × 256 MB = 2.0 GB per rank

(…continued)

4. Peak activation memory (dispatch buffers) per rank:
   input buffer = capacity × local_experts × d_model × 2 bytes
                = 80 × 8 × 4096 × 2 = 5.2 MB
   expert_output buffer = same = 5.2 MB
   CombineBuffer = n_tokens × d_model × 2 = 2048 × 4096 × 2 = 16.8 MB
   total activations per rank ≈ 27 MB (dominated by CombineBuffer at full batch)

5. All-to-all bandwidth per rank (uniform routing):
   send_per_rank = capacity × d_model × 2 bytes = 80 × 4096 × 2 = 655 KB per peer
   total_send = (W-1) × 655 KB = 7 × 655 KB ≈ 4.6 MB per forward pass
   at 100 GbE (12.5 GB/s): a2a latency = 4.6 MB / 12.5 GB/s = 0.37 ms
   at NVLink 300 GB/s: a2a latency ≈ 0.015 ms (compute-bound regime)

(…continued)

6. Expert GEMM time per rank (BF16 CPU reference):
   each expert GEMM: [80, 4096] × [4096, 16384] → 80 × 4096 × 16384 × 2 FLOPs
                   = 10.7 GFLOP per expert per direction × 2 (in + out) = 21.4 GFLOP
   local GEMMs: 8 experts × 21.4 GFLOP = 171 GFLOP
   at 1 TFLOP/s CPU (typical AVX-512 BF16 peak): 171 GFLOP / 1000 GFLOP/s = 0.17 s
   at A100 GPU (77.6 TFLOP/s BF16): 171 GFLOP / 77,600 GFLOP/s = 2.2 ms per forward

7. Overlap efficiency target:
   comm_bound: a2a = 0.37 ms; compute = 2.2 ms → compute dominates (good for overlap)
   ideal overlap: hide full a2a behind GEMM → 0% a2a on critical path
   actual overhead (estimated from Phase 8): 10–20% residual comm exposure
   effective forward time ≈ 2.2 ms + 0.2 × 0.37 ms ≈ 2.3 ms (vs 2.57 ms serial)

8. Gate logit GEMM:
   [n_tokens, d_model] × [d_model, E] = [2048, 4096] × [4096, 64]
   FLOPs = 2048 × 4096 × 64 × 2 = 1.1 GFLOP (negligible vs expert GEMM)

(…continued)

9. Aux loss memory overhead:
   AuxLossState: [E] importance + [E] load = 2 × 64 × 4 bytes = 512 bytes
   z_loss: scalar = 4 bytes
   Total aux overhead: < 1 KB — negligible

10. Expert weight activation recompute cost (Phase 6 backward):
    No recompute: store all 8 expert activations = 8 × [80, 16384] × 2 bytes = 21 MB
    Recompute: re-execute w_in GEMM during backward = 21.4 GFLOP × 8 = 171 GFLOP
    Trade-off: 21 MB VRAM vs 2.2 ms re-compute at A100 — storage wins at A100 scale

11. Summary (A100 GPU, BF16, EP=8, E=64, n=2048, k=2, capacity_factor=1.25):
    Weight VRAM per rank:    2.0 GB
    Peak activation VRAM:   ~27 MB
    Gate logit time:         <0.1 ms
    Expert GEMM time:        2.2 ms
    A2A latency (100 GbE):   0.37 ms
    Effective forward:       ~2.3 ms with overlap
    Token drop rate at 3σ:   ~0% (capacity_factor=1.25 provides sufficient headroom)

Hard Questions

  1. If you set capacity_factor=1.0 with k=2 and E=64, what is the expected token drop rate at uniform routing for a batch of 2048 tokens, and which §§ provide the formula to compute it? Requires synthesis of §4.2 (capacity formula) and §§ Back-of-Envelope Capacity Model. Answer requires knowing that uniform routing produces Poisson-like per-expert load with mean=64 and computing the tail probability beyond capacity=64. Resolving §: §4.2, §7 I2, §§ Back-of-Envelope item 1.

  2. Why does the capacity formula use ceil() rather than floor(), and what invariant would be violated if you used floor()? Requires understanding the token conservation invariant (I1) and the capacity invariant (I2). With floor, a batch where n_tokens×k is exactly divisible by E leaves zero slack; any non-uniform routing causes drops on at least one expert. Resolving §: §7 I1/I2, §4.2.

  3. Trace the data path of a single token from input tensor through moe-gate → moe-dispatch → moe-comm → moe-expert → moe-comm → moe-dispatch → output, naming the exact Rust type at each crate boundary. Requires familiarity with §11 core types, §3 architecture, Phase 0.2 and Phase 6.2. Resolving §: §3.1, §11, Phase 0.2, Phase 6.2.

  4. If two tokens have identical logits for all 64 experts, which two experts does the top-2 router assign them to, and is the assignment identical across AMD and Intel CPUs? Requires understanding the tie-break rule (I3) and IEEE 754 determinism (§5.4 IEEE 754 reference). Answer: ExpertId 0 and 1, always, because the tie-break is ascending ExpertId after deterministic sort; IEEE 754 guarantees identical f32 comparison results for identical bit patterns. Resolving §: §7 I3, §6.2 (P2), §5.4 (IEEE 754).

  5. What is the mathematical relationship between routing entropy, token drop rate, and aux loss at training convergence, and which §§ define each quantity? Requires synthesis of §6.1 (P1 token conservation), §§ Mental Models & First Principles MM1/MM3, §7 I8 (aux loss monotonicity), and §4 system constraints. Resolving §: §6.1, §6.4, §7 I8, §§ Back-of-Envelope items 9 and 11.

  6. If rank 3 in an 8-rank EP group fails mid-forward-pass while the all-to-all is in progress, what is the exact sequence of events in moe-comm and moe-runtime, and which invariants must still hold? Requires familiarity with Phase 11.2 (HeartbeatMonitor, failover a2a), I1 (token conservation via residual passthrough on failure), I10 (A2A symmetric counts must be rebuilt). Resolving §: Phase 11.2, §7 I1/I10, §§ Failure Modes & Recovery (rank failure subsection).

  7. Why does expert-choice routing require the full [n_tokens × num_experts] score matrix to be materialized before dispatch, while token-choice routing does not, and what is the latency implication at n_tokens=4096, E=128? Requires understanding MM5 (Mental Models & First Principles) and Phase 9.2 expert_choice sync barrier. Score matrix = 4096 × 128 × 4 bytes = 2 MB; must be gathered from all ranks before column-wise topk. Resolving §: Phase 9.2, §§ Alternative Architectures, §§ Mental Models & First Principles MM5.

  8. What happens to the AuxLossState if a forward pass has zero tokens (n_tokens=0), and what should the backward pass return for z_loss gradient in that case? Requires Phase 4.2 (AuxLossState with zero tokens), §7 I8 (aux loss monotonicity — zero inputs → zero loss), Phase 13.2 (differential harness edge cases). Resolving §: Phase 4.2, §7 I8, Phase 1 edge cases section.

  9. If you increase world_size from 4 to 8 with E=64, k=2, n_tokens=2048, keeping capacity_factor=1.25, what changes in per-rank VRAM, a2a bandwidth, and expected drop rate? Requires §§ Back-of-Envelope (items 2–5) applied twice and understanding the a2a bandwidth formula. Expert weights halve; a2a bandwidth per rank: (W-1) × capacity × d_model × dtype — increases as W grows but message count per peer shrinks. Resolving §: §§ Back-of-Envelope, §4, §§ Scalability Analysis (Network Fanout).

  10. Why is the combine step not simply a sum over expert outputs, and what exact check would reveal a bug where the gate weights were not renormalized after a token was partially dropped? Requires understanding §7 I5 (combine correctness), §6.1 (P1 token conservation), and Phase 2.2 (combine_with_residual). Partial drop means fewer than k experts accepted the token; if combine sums all k gate weights regardless, the output is scaled down. Resolving §: §7 I5, Phase 2.2, §6.1 (P1).

  11. Which crate owns the unsafe keyword for GEMM FFI, why is it confined there, and what ASAN/Miri test validates it? Requires ADR-04 (cblas-sys + cudarc), §6.7 (P7 FFI safety), Phase 5.2 (gemm_ffi.rs). Answer: moe-expert/gemm_ffi.rs exclusively; isolated so AddressSanitizer CI target can instrument only that crate boundary. Resolving §: §6.7, Phase 5.2, ADR-18.

  12. How does the double-buffer state machine in moe-sched prevent a data race between the a2a receive and the combine step without a mutex? Requires Phase 8.2 (doublebuf.rs ping-pong), §7 I6 (overlap liveness), §6.8 (P8 explicit overlap). The event-based protocol — compute stream waits on a2a-done event before reading receive buffer — eliminates the race without a lock. Resolving §: Phase 8.2, §7 I6, §6.8.

  13. If W_g (gate projection matrix) is initialized to all zeros, what routing behavior emerges, what does the aux loss report, and how does the z-loss prevent logit explosion in subsequent training steps? Requires Phase 1.2 (topk_indices_deterministic tie-break), §7 I9 (gate logit safety), Phase 4.2 (z-loss formula).
