Zero W_g → all logits zero → tie-break assigns experts 0..k-1 to every token → load catastrophically imbalanced → aux loss non-zero immediately → z-loss = 0 (logits = 0 → logsumexp = log(E)) initially but prevents logit runaway as gradients flow. Resolving §: Phase 1.2, Phase 4.2, §7 I9.

  14. What is the exact byte layout of the safetensors header for a checkpoint with 8 expert weight tensors (w_in, w_out per expert), and how does moe-cli validate it before mmap? Requires ADR-09 (safetensors spec), Phase 5.2 (weight_loader.rs validation), §5.4 (SafeTensors spec reference). Header is JSON with dtype, shape, data_offsets per tensor; validation checks magic bytes, header length, tensor count, expected expert names. Resolving §: ADR-09, Phase 5.2, §5.4.

  15. If you reduce capacity_factor from 1.25 to 0.8 on a live system, what happens to token drop rate, training loss, and routing entropy over the next 1000 training steps, and which telemetry counters would you watch? Requires §§ Operational Readiness (alert thresholds), §§ Back-of-Envelope item 1, §7 I8 (aux loss response). Drop rate spikes immediately (overflow); training loss may degrade or recover depending on aux loss coefficient; routing entropy usually increases as gate compensates; watch moe_drop_rate, moe_routing_entropy, moe_aux_loss. Resolving §: §§ Operational Readiness, §§ Back-of-Envelope, §7 I1/I8.

  16. Why does the InProc transport use crossbeam bounded channels rather than tokio channels, and what would happen if the channel were unbounded? Requires ADR-07 (crossbeam SPSC for InProc), §6.5 (P5 explicit backpressure). Bounded: backpressure visible as MoeError::QueueFull; unbounded: producer can outrun consumer indefinitely, arena grows without bound, OOM crash with no recoverable error. Resolving §: ADR-07, §6.5, Phase 7.2.

  17. How does the per-rank expert ownership formula expert_id % world_size interact with a non-power-of-two expert count like E=48, W=8, and what load imbalance results? Requires §6.9 (P9 EP consistency), §7 I4, §§ Scalability Analysis sharding section. Expert distribution: ranks 0–7 get ceil(48/8)=6 or floor(48/8)=6 experts (exact division here); for E=50, W=8: ranks 0–1 get 7 experts, ranks 2–7 get 6 — a 16% imbalance in compute, handled by capacity_factor and telemetry alert. Resolving §: §6.9, §7 I4, §§ Scalability Analysis.

  18. What is the worst-case memory allocation during a forward pass if one expert receives 3× its capacity due to a routing collapse, and how does the code prevent this from causing heap growth? Pre-allocated arena-backed buffers: expert input buffer is sized to capacity at layer init, not dynamically. Overflow tokens are dropped before scatter; they never reach the expert buffer. The arena cursor never advances beyond the pre-computed high-water mark. Resolving §: §6.3 (P3), §7 I2, Phase 0.2 (Arena), Phase 3.2 (capacity.rs reject logic).

  19. If the z-loss coefficient is set to 0.0, what is the theoretical failure mode and under which training configuration is it most likely to manifest? Zero z-loss → no logit magnitude regularization → gate logits can grow arbitrarily large during training → softmax becomes numerically degenerate (one expert gets weight ≈ 1.0 for all tokens) → routing collapse into a single expert → aux loss fails to correct because load term is already 1.0 for that expert. Most likely with large learning rate + small aux_loss_coeff + deep model (many MoE layers). Resolving §: Phase 4.2 (z-loss), §7 I9, §§ Failure Modes & Recovery.

  20. How would you modify the DispatchPlan to support fractional token drops (dropping only one of k assignments for an overflowed token rather than the full token)? Requires synthesis of Phase 2.2 (plan.rs), §7 I5 (combine correctness), §6.1 (P1 token conservation). Partial drop requires a per-assignment dropped flag (not a per-token BitSet), a renormalization step in combine (re-normalize remaining k-1 weights to sum to 1.0), and a new invariant: sum(assigned_weights[t]) ∈ {0.0, 1.0} for all t. Tradeoff: eliminates full token loss at high capacity pressure but makes combine more expensive. Resolving §: Phase 2.2, §7 I1/I5, §11.7 DispatchPlan.

Alternative Architectures

Alternative 1 — Block-Sparse GEMM (Megablocks-style)

Token batch (sorted by assigned expert):
[T0,T1,T4,T7] [T2,T3,T5] [T6,T8,T9] [T11]
 Expert 0       Expert 1   Expert 2   Expert 3

Block-sparse layout: experts padded to tile boundary; GEMM on sorted block:
[ block_e0 | block_e1 | block_e2 | block_e3 ]
  ≤ tile    ≤ tile     ≤ tile     ≤ tile
All in one cuSPARSE / Triton block-sparse kernel call

Regime where this wins: very large E (E ≥ 256), high k (k ≥ 4), and a GPU with good block-sparse kernel support (A100+ with Triton). Eliminates per-expert GEMM overhead at the cost of sorted-token permutation. Real system: Megablocks (Gale 2022, MLSys 2023). Why rejected here: requires CUDA-native block-sparse kernel; not portable to CPU reference path; grouped GEMM via cblas-sys achieves the same semantic on CPU without the kernel dependency.

Alternative 2 — Expert-Choice with Column-Major DispatchPlan

Score matrix [E x T]:
         T0    T1    T2    T3    T4
Expert0  0.9   0.3   0.7   0.2   0.5
Expert1  0.6   0.8   0.1   0.9   0.4
Expert2  0.2   0.5   0.8   0.3   0.7
Expert3  0.7   0.4   0.3   0.6   0.1

Each expert selects top-c tokens (c = capacity):
Expert0 → [T0, T2], Expert1 → [T1, T3], Expert2 → [T2, T4], Expert3 → [T0, T3]
                                         ^conflict^            ^conflict^
Conflict resolution: token keeps assignment from highest-weight expert only

Regime where this wins: training workloads where load balance is more important than inference latency and no token-drop tolerance exists. Expert-choice provides perfect expert utilization at the cost of the score-matrix barrier. Real system: Zhou et al. 2022 (NeurIPS 2022). Why rejected as default: the full [n_tokens × E] matrix barrier adds latency that token-choice avoids; implemented as a Phase 9 variant but not the default routing mode.

Alternative 3 — All-Dense MoE (k=E, no dispatch)

input [n_tokens, d_model]
         |
  [gate] W_g -> softmax over all E -> gate_weights [n_tokens, E]
         |
  [experts] all E experts compute simultaneously:
  expert_out[e] = FFN_e(input) for all e in 0..E
         |
  [combine] output = sum_e(gate_weights[e] * expert_out[e])

Regime where this wins: small E (E ≤ 4), very small d_ffn, or when interpretability of all-expert contributions is needed (research ablation). Eliminates all dispatch/a2a machinery; full batch through every expert. Real system: "soft MoE" (Puigcerver 2023, arXiv 2308.00951). Why rejected: compute cost = O(E × n_tokens × d_ffn); for E=64 this is 64× a dense FFN — defeats the purpose of MoE sparsity; only viable as an integration test baseline (capacity=inf, k=E).

Alternative 4 — Hierarchical EP (Two-Tier All-to-All)

World: 4 nodes × 8 GPUs = 32 ranks; E = 64 experts

Tier 1 (intra-node, NVLink): ranks 0–7 exchange tokens for local experts 0–15
Tier 2 (inter-node, IB):     node leaders exchange tokens for remote experts 16–63

Reduces inter-node bandwidth by: (intra-node experts / E) fraction
At E=64, 16 local experts: 75% of tokens stay intra-node (NVLink)

Regime where this wins: large E (≥ 64), high world_size (≥ 16), heterogeneous fabric (NVLink intra-node + InfiniBand inter-node). Dramatically reduces expensive inter-node bandwidth at the cost of a more complex two-level EpGroup topology. Real system: DeepSpeed-MoE hierarchical EP (Rajbhandari 2022). Why rejected as default: two-tier EpGroup requires a topology-aware Transport that knows node boundaries; adds significant implementation complexity; deferred to the §§ Deferred Features Register (un-defer trigger: world_size ≥ 16 and inter-node bandwidth < 100 Gb/s).

Deferred Features Register

Feature Anchor Building Phase Un-defer Trigger
Hierarchical two-tier EP (intra-node NVLink + inter-node IB) §§ Alternative Architectures Alt-4; ADR-08 Deferred after Phase 5 world_size ≥ 16 AND inter-node bandwidth < 100 Gb/s measured in bench
Dynamic EP resize without restart, incl. hot expert-weight reload / zero-downtime reconfiguration (world_size change live) P10 (Tutel Adaptive Parallelism); Phase 6 (ep_group.rs) Deferred after Phase 6 operator requirement for elastic GPU cluster; EpGroup::resize() API specified
Non-uniform expert placement (FlexMoE expert-to-rank registry) P12 (FlexMoE); Phase 5 ep_group.rs Deferred after Phase 9 heterogeneous hardware with non-uniform expert popularity measured in telemetry
Tensor parallelism inside each expert FFN (intra-expert TP) §§ Scalability Analysis sharding section Deferred after Phase 9 d_ff > 65536 (expert GEMM exceeds single-GPU memory bandwidth ceiling)
Expert activation recompute (selective rematerialization) P13 (Korthikanti 2022); Phase 6 backward.rs Deferred after Phase 6 peak activation VRAM > 80% GPU HBM budget at batch_size target
Fp8 E5M2 quantized GEMM via Transformer Engine Phase 10 (quant.rs); ADR-04 Deferred after Phase 10 Transformer Engine available in target CUDA environment AND fp8 error < 1% vs bf16
Adaptive routing (run-time k adjustment based on load) P8 (Tutel Adaptive); Phase 9 routing variants Deferred after Phase 12 auxiliary loss fails to converge within 1000 steps under current static k
Expert popularity-weighted capacity_factor per expert §4 system constraints; Phase 3 capacity.rs Deferred after Phase 11 telemetry shows > 3 experts consistently near 100% utilization while others < 30%
INT4 quantized expert weights (int8/fp8 already built in Phase 10) Phase 10 (quant.rs) Deferred after Phase 10 INT4 hardware kernel support available AND accuracy loss < 2% vs int8 baseline on conformance fixtures
Dynamic expert routing with learned capacity (runtime-adjusted top-k / capacity_factor) Phase 9 routing variants Deferred after Phase 9 telemetry shows static capacity_factor headroom unused by > 20% across 3 consecutive releases
Multi-node TCP/RDMA transport (production UCX/RDMA path beyond the in-proc/MPI stub) Phase 7 (transport.rs; Ucx stub); ADR-08 Deferred after Phase 7 multi-node deployment target confirmed AND UCX Rust bindings graduate out of immature status
Kernel autotuning / tile-size search, incl. block-sparse GEMM tiling (Megablocks-style) Phase 5 (grouped_gemm.rs); §§ Alternative Architectures Alt-1 Deferred after Phase 5 target GPU is A100+ with Triton/cuSPARSE block-sparse kernel support
Micro-batch pipeline depth > 2 (3+ stage comm/compute overlap) Phase 8 (moe-sched doublebuf.rs) Deferred after Phase 8 telemetry shows overlap_efficiency < 0.7 sustained at pipeline depth 2 under production batch sizes

Glossary

Term Definition
all-to-all (a2a) Collective op where every EP rank sends a distinct shard to every other rank; inverse applied after expert compute; bottleneck in EP scalability.
aux load-balance loss Differentiable penalty = sum_e(f_e * p_e) × num_experts added to total loss; f_e = fraction tokens routed to e, p_e = mean gate probability for e; drives uniform utilization without explicit dropping.
AuxLossState Runtime accumulator { importance: Vec<f32>, load: Vec<f32> } holding per-expert f_e and p_e needed to emit aux loss; collected inside moe-gate per forward pass.
batched GEMM Single cuBLAS/BLAS call driving multiple independent matrix multiplications sharing one dispatch; used when multiple expert token batches can share a kernel launch.
capacity Hard upper bound on tokens an expert processes per forward: Capacity = ceil(capacity_factor × n_tokens × k / num_experts); enforced inside moe-dispatch.
capacity factor Scalar multiplier (≥1.0) controlling expert buffer size relative to ideal load; 1.0 = exactly ideal, 1.25 = 25 % overflow headroom; tunable without recompile via config.
CombineBuffer Typed handle { buf: Tensor, weights: Tensor, expert_slots: Vec<Range<usize>> } holding post-expert outputs aligned to permutation order for weighted scatter-add.
comm/compute overlap Scheduling pattern where all-to-all sends and expert GEMMs execute on independent device streams; requires double-buffering of token/weight tensors; primary latency optimization.
dense baseline Reference path with capacity_factor = ∞ and k = num_experts routing all tokens to all experts; output equals standard FFN; used for correctness differential tests.
dispatch Phase where tokens are permuted into contiguous per-expert buffers following RoutingDecision; includes capacity check, drop decision, and scatter into DispatchPlan.perm.
DispatchPlan { perm: Vec<u32>, per_expert_counts: Vec<u32>, dropped: BitSet, capacity: Capacity } — complete description of the token→expert mapping for one forward pass.
double-buffering Ping-pong pattern using two DispatchPlan/CombineBuffer pairs so stage N+1 dispatch overlaps stage N expert compute; managed by moe-sched.
drop policy Rule for which tokens are evicted when expert capacity is exceeded; canonical = drop lowest gate weight first; deterministic tie-break by TokenIdx.
dropped token Token that exceeds expert capacity and is excluded from expert compute; passes through unmodified via residual (output = input); tracked in DispatchPlan.dropped BitSet.
DType Enum { F32, BF16, F16, I8 } stored in Tensor handle; controls GEMM dispatch and quantized weight dequant path; F32 mandatory for gate logits.
EP rank / EP world Index of this process within the expert-parallel process group (EpGroup); expert e lives on rank e % world_size; determines which weight shard is local.
EpGroup { rank: u32, world_size: u32, transport: Transport } — identifies the expert-parallel collective group; scoped per MoeLayer instance; transport is In-proc
expert One FFN sub-network FFN(x) = activation(x × w_in) × w_out; weight matrices sharded across EP ranks; selected by gate routing for a subset of tokens.
expert-choice routing Router variant where each expert selects its top-C tokens (budget-controlled) instead of tokens selecting experts; guarantees exact capacity; no token dropping; from Zhou 2022.
expert parallelism (EP) Model-parallelism strategy placing disjoint expert weight shards on distinct devices/ranks; tokens dispatched to owning rank via all-to-all; expert compute is fully local.
expert utilization Metric = tokens_processed_by_expert / capacity; per-expert value in [0, 1]; >0.95 sustained triggers overload alert; imbalance detectable via routing entropy.
ExpertId Newtype ExpertId(u32); valid range [0, num_experts); used as array index in RoutingDecision and DispatchPlan.
ExpertWeights { w_in: Tensor, w_out: Tensor, sharded_across: EpGroup } — storage descriptor for one expert's FFN parameters; sharding axis and dtype tracked for GEMM dispatch.
GateLogits [n_tokens × num_experts] f32 tensor; raw pre-softmax scores from W_g × x; input to top-k selection; stored F32 regardless of model precision.
gating / router Linear projection W_g ∈ ℝ^{d×E} mapping token embedding to expert scores; followed by top-k + softmax in moe-gate; may include noise injection for noisy top-k.
grouped GEMM Kernel executing variable-length GEMMs for all experts in one launch using per-expert M-dim offsets; avoids kernel-launch overhead; primary path in moe-expert.
invariant Hard correctness property that must hold across all code paths; violations are fatal (panic or MoeError); tested by differential harness and property-based tests.
K / top-k Number of experts each token is routed to; typically 1 or 2 in published models; controls compute density and memory bandwidth per forward pass.
load-balance loss See aux load-balance loss; sometimes called importance loss or load loss depending on paper; both terms resolve to same AuxLossState fields.
micro-batch Subdivision of a forward-pass batch used to pipeline dispatch, comm, expert, and combine stages across sequential micro-batches; controlled by moe-sched.
MoeError Exhaustive error enum covering: ShapeMismatch, CapacityOverflow, TransportError, GemmBackendError, ConfigError, WeightLoadError; no panics in library paths.
MoeLayer::forward Public entry point: fn forward(&self, x: Tensor, aux: &mut AuxLossState) -> Result<Tensor, MoeError>; orchestrated by moe-runtime; only public surface for inference.
noisy top-k Router variant adding N(0,1) × softplus(W_noise × x) to logits before top-k selection; improves load balance during training; noise disabled at inference.
permutation DispatchPlan.perm = integer array mapping token-in-batch position to contiguous position in per-expert buffer; used for scatter (dispatch) and gather (combine).
residual passthrough Identity path for dropped tokens: output[dropped] = input[dropped]; ensures token conservation invariant even when capacity is exceeded.
routing determinism Invariant: identical input + seed → bit-identical RoutingDecision and DispatchPlan; enforced by deterministic top-k tie-break on ExpertId.
routing entropy H = -Σ_e p_e × log(p_e) over per-expert load fractions; maximum at uniform routing; low entropy signals expert collapse; emitted as moe-telemetry gauge.
RoutingDecision Per-token struct { topk: [(ExpertId, f32); K] } where f32 = softmax-normalized gate weight; weights sum to 1.0 across K entries.
scatter-gather Low-level memory ops implementing permutation: scatter fills per-expert contiguous buffer from token batch; gather reverses after expert compute for combine.
shared expert Expert(s) that receive ALL tokens unconditionally alongside top-k sparse routing; used in DeepSeekMoE / Qwen-MoE architectures; modeled as k+S routing in DispatchPlan.
softmax-over-topk Renormalization of gate weights to the selected K experts after top-k masking, so weights sum to 1; preserves gradient magnitude; canonical combine weighting.
ST-MoE z-loss Penalty L_z = α × log(Σ_e exp(logit_e))^2 stabilizing gate logit magnitude; prevents logit explosion; from Zoph 2022 ST-MoE paper.
token conservation Invariant: every input token appears exactly once in the output; non-dropped tokens via expert paths; dropped tokens via residual passthrough.
TokenIdx Newtype TokenIdx(u32); position in the input batch [0, n_tokens); used in BitSet for dropped-token tracking and permutation validation.
transport Collective communication backend abstraction in moe-comm: In-proc (shared-memory channel for single-process EP), NCCL (multi-GPU), TCP-fallback.
z-loss See ST-MoE z-loss; coefficient α typically 1e-3; added to training loss; not emitted at inference but state must be zero-able without recompile.

Appendix B — Milestone Summary

Phase Deliverable Duration Key Risks Exit Criteria
0 — Skeleton Workspace scaffold; all 10 crates typed; Tensor handle; single dense FFN expert; top-1 route-one-token-and-combine smoke test (no comm, capacity=inf) 1–2 wk MSRV 1.78 compat with SmallVec/bitset crates; arena alignment cargo test --workspace 100% pass; smoke E2E ≤1e-5 vs numpy; clippy zero warnings
1 — Gating network Dense top-k router, softmax-over-topk, deterministic tie-break, router logits 1 wk Tie-break determinism across platforms (IEEE 754) Σ weights within 1e-5 of 1.0; tie-break 100/100 identical same seed
2 — Token dispatch + combine Permutation/scatter-gather, per-expert contiguous buffers, weighted combine (single device) 1 wk Off-by-one in permutation build; combine renormalization bugs Token conservation ≤1e-5 vs reference; residual passthrough bit-exact
3 — Capacity and drop policy Capacity factor, per-expert capacity, deterministic token dropping, overflow handling 1 wk Drop ordering correctness at capacity boundary Capacity invariant holds for all experts; drop determinism 100/100 identical
4 — Load balancing Importance/load auxiliary loss, router z-loss, expert-utilization + routing-entropy metrics 1 wk z-loss gradient correctness; aux loss overhead L_aux thresholds met; routing_entropy = log(num_experts) at uniform routing
5 — Batched expert compute Grouped/batched GEMM (FFI), ragged batch + padding, FFN (GEMM→act→GEMM) 2 wk BLAS/cuBLAS ABI across platforms; grouped GEMM shape edge cases GEMM ≥50 GFLOPS (CPU ref); zero heap allocs in run_all_experts
6 — Expert parallelism (multi-process) EP process groups, expert-weight sharding, all-to-all dispatch + combine across ranks 2 wk Count-symmetry bugs across ranks; weight-shard boundary errors Token conservation across 4-rank InProc; EP consistency test passes
7 — All-to-all communication engine Transport abstraction, pairwise/ring a2a, serialization, flow control + backpressure 1.5 wk Backpressure deadlock; malformed-wire handling Pairwise vs ring byte-identical; InProc a2a throughput >2 GB/s
8 — Comm/compute overlap scheduler Streams/queues, double-buffering, micro-batch pipelining, prefetch 2 wk Loom races in double-buffer swap; pipeline dependency-graph cycles Overlapped output == serial output; overlap_efficiency ≥0.4 at M=2
9 — Advanced routing Expert-choice routing, noisy top-k, hash/random routing, shared experts 1.5 wk Expert-choice capacity guarantee under adversarial inputs All routing variants pass differential; shared-expert token conservation
10 — Quantization and memory management Int8/fp8 expert weights, weight offload/paging, activation memory pools 1.5–2 wk Quantization accuracy loss; paging latency spikes Int8/fp8 relative error <1.5%/<3%; zero heap allocs in forward hot path
11 — Fault tolerance and determinism Rank-failure handling, expert-replica fallback, deterministic replay from routing seed 1.5 wk Heartbeat/failover races; replay log divergence Failure detection ≤300 ms; 1000-pass replay bit-identical
12 — Observability and profiling Per-expert utilization, routing entropy, drop rate, comm-vs-compute time, flamegraph workflow 1 wk Telemetry overhead on hot path; OTLP export backpressure Telemetry overhead ≤1%; OTLP zero dropped spans at 100 rps
13 — Conformance, differential testing, fuzzing, benchmarks Validation vs dense reference and Tutel/Megablocks semantics; throughput/latency benches 1–1.5 wk Flaky benchmarks; fuzz false positives 0 violations over 10k fuzz iterations; throughput regression <5%
